# CoveyKeep — Repo Operational Guide: history (moved from `CLAUDE.md`)

**Version:** 1.2.0
**Last Updated:** 2026-10-29
**Source:** Moved from the code-root `CLAUDE.md` v1.87.0 (`23bc47d`) at its 1.88.0 (2026-10-28) — the sixth doc audit's condensing proposal (Hub `covey-keep/audits/2026-10-28-doc-audit.md` 1.2.0 §19), applied at Steven's go. The `**Source:**` line and 87 changelog entries — versions 1.84.0 down to 1.0.0 (2026-10-25 back to 2026-08-17) — moved here byte for byte and in their original order.
**Audience:** Any session that needs why the code-root `CLAUDE.md` changed, and when. Not auto-loaded: Claude Code loads `CLAUDE.md` alone.

**Changelog:**
- **1.2.0** (2026-10-29): One section appended at the code-root `CLAUDE.md` 1.91.0, the sixth doc audit's last open item: the three Deploys bullets — the publication gate (CK-41), Groups (CK-45) and the sweep (CK-59) — as they stood in v1.90.0, before each was rewritten to its current state. They carried no amendment mark, so 1.89.0's rewrite had not reached them. Verbatim and in their original order; above them only this entry, the version and date lines and the paragraph saying what 1.2.0 added are new.
- **1.1.0** (2026-10-28): Two sections appended at the code-root `CLAUDE.md` 1.89.0, the last piece of the sixth doc audit's condensing proposal (§19): the directory map as it stood in v1.88.0 (177 lines, 178,813 bytes), replaced there by one line per tracked file, and the nine Deploys bullets that carried a restatement or another inline amendment (23,880 bytes), as they stood before each was rewritten to its current state. Both verbatim and in their original order; above them only this entry, the version line and the paragraph saying what 1.1.0 added are new. 453,572 → 658,270 bytes.
- **1.0.0** (2026-10-28): Initial creation. The live file keeps its newest entries and a one-line pointer to this file.

**Why this file exists, and what it is not.** `CLAUDE.md` is loaded into every Claude Code session in the code root, and these 87 entries were 53.9% of it. **This is not a summary.** Every entry below is the text that stood in `CLAUDE.md`, unaltered and in its original order, newest first. The move was proved before it was made: the live file with its two pointers swapped back for this text equals `CLAUDE.md` at `23bc47d` byte for byte, and every line here appears once here and nowhere in the live file.

**What 1.1.0 added.** Two sections at the end, moved from `CLAUDE.md` at its 1.89.0 (2026-10-28), the last step of the same proposal: the directory map as it stood in v1.88.0, one entry per folder carrying its files' descriptions with their history phase by phase, which `CLAUDE.md` replaced with one line per tracked file saying what the file is now; and the nine Deploys bullets that carried a *Restated at CK-NN* chain or another inline amendment, as they stood before `CLAUDE.md` rewrote each to its current state. They are verbatim too: every line of the old map and of the nine bullets is in those sections once, in its original order, and nowhere in the live file except the map's two fence lines, proved against `CLAUDE.md` at `4056268`.

**What 1.2.0 added.** One section at the end, moved from `CLAUDE.md` at its 1.91.0 (2026-10-29): the three Deploys bullets that carried no amendment mark and still described their phase's moment, as they stood before `CLAUDE.md` rewrote each to its current state. Verbatim too, proved against `CLAUDE.md` at `ef8eee3`.

## Changelog entries 1.84.0 down to 1.0.0, as they stood in `CLAUDE.md` v1.87.0

- **1.84.0** (2026-10-25): Phase CK-77.2 — **two requests at once get one answer: the link, the invitations, the tags and the sign-in** (the five unbackstopped unique writes CK-77.1's STEP 1 enumerated, plus the planning session's own finding reading the sign-in handler for the kickoff; `reference/backend/api-reference.md` 1.40.2 — each route's race outcome and the /auth/verify sentence; **backend only: `api/group_links.py`, `api/invitations.py`, `api/group_invitations.py`, `api/media.py`, `api/auth.py` and a new `tests/test_double_requests.py` — no migration, head stays 0034, the verifier at 361 unchanged (361 / 0 locally), `alembic check` clean, nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, `render.yaml` untouched**; **685 backend tests, 11 new**, no existing test edited; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT CLASS, READ COMMITTED's**: a statement cannot see rows another transaction has not committed when it starts, so two requests that each "clear the old, then insert the new" each clear only what they can see and then insert beside each other. **STEP 1 CONFIRMED THE TABLE ROW BY ROW**: `make_link` (CK-75) revoked and `add`ed, the insert flushed at the commit with no `try` — `uq_group_links_live`, a 500; `create_invitation` (CK-25) and `create_group_invitation` (CK-75) superseded and `add`ed the same way — the two live-destination partial uniques, a 500 at the commit, BEFORE the send (confirmed: both routes send only after their commit, so the loser mailed nothing); `patch_media` (CK-39) took no lock, so two tag saves at once deleted what each could see — an identical list a 500 on `uq_media_tags_media_id_tag`, two lists their union, `Tommy` beside `tommy` the case twin the verifier forbids; `verify` (CK-5, CK-12) flushed a new person with no guard — `uq_people_email`, a 500 — and consumed the token by `row.consumed_at = now` on a row read earlier, so one link opened twice at once was redeemed twice. **One correction to the table's last row**: for a NEW address, one link opened twice gives the 500 only when both openings read no person; staged after the token SELECT (the kickoff's moment) the second opening FOUND the person the first created and minted a second session — the same defect as the existing-person case, not the 500. **THE FIXES, each by the two CK-77.1 rules, no shared wrapper, no retry, nothing caught, nothing rolled back**: the three creates write their row as an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING` on their partial unique (the `put_place` idiom) and, when it wrote nothing, read the row that holds — **201 with the SAME link**, never a fresh one the other request revokes a moment later; **201 with the one pending row and NOTHING SENT** — the loser's token was never stored and mailing it would send a dead link, so one email in all; **`patch_media` locks the photograph's row** (`with_for_update(of=Media)`, `_review_row`'s shape — THE ONE LOCK THIS PHASE ADDS, there to keep a replacement whole, not to win a race: no `ON CONFLICT` makes a delete-then-insert atomic), so two saves take turns and the second's whole list holds; **`verify` consumes FIRST by a guarded `UPDATE … WHERE consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now` with its rowcount checked**, before the person is looked up or written (rowcount 0 → the uniform error; a link signs in once), and writes a new person `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (email) DO NOTHING RETURNING`, the loser deleting the account row it had just inserted (the verifier's `accounts == people + organizations`), re-reading the person by the address captured from the token row and signing in as them — no ToS row from the loser, the request that created the person recorded it. **THE EMPTY READ, decided** (the kickoff left it): when the row that held was withdrawn — or, for an invitation, accepted — between the loser's insert and its read, the three creates answer **409 `withdrawn_meanwhile`** (`invitations.py::WITHDRAWN_MEANWHILE` / `_withdrawn_meanwhile`, one code the three share; another try makes the row) — vanishingly rare, pinned on all three by a two-stage race, never a 500; `verify`'s analogue (the winning person gone again before the re-read) answers the uniform error and the session's close rolls the consume back. **RED FIRST, EVERY RACE STAGED, NEVER TIMED** (`tests/test_double_requests.py`, 11): the three creates hooked on the route's own supersession UPDATE by KIND (`_dml(Update, table)` — the reaper's DELETE is the first DML on each table, so a kind-blind predicate fires at the reaper) with the competitor's live row committed from a second session, and a second stage on the route's INSERT for the empty-read cases; the two sign-in races hooked on the person SELECT (two links) and the token SELECT (one link twice); **the tags with a HELD competitor** — a second session locks the row, deletes, inserts its list and flushes without committing, the route runs as a task, and a THIRD connection watches `pg_locks` for the route's ungranted lock or its finish (bounded, never a fixed sleep) before the competitor commits; two variants, the identical list and `Tommy` against `tommy`, the same test valid red and green because the fixed route waits on the row lock in both. Against `710b6d8` all eleven failed on exactly their defect — `UniqueViolationError` on `uq_group_links_live` (twice), `uq_gathering_invitations_pending_live_destination` (twice), `uq_group_invitations_pending_live_destination` (twice), `uq_media_tags_media_id_tag` and `uq_people_email`; `['tommy', 'Tommy']` stored; a token redirect where the uniform error was due (twice) — then green with the code changed and the tests unedited. **STEP 1'S TWO ENUMERATIONS**: wholesale replacements under `app/` — the tags (nothing serialised two writers unless the caption changed; FIXED), the companions (`put_rsvp`'s own write on the RSVP row — the INSERT and flush on a first answer, the `updated_at` UPDATE autoflushed by the companions' delete on a change — holds the row lock first; untouched), and the derivative rows at publish (`services/ingest.py` step 4 — `_settle`'s guarded UPDATE on the media row runs first and a lost claim returns before the delete, so two workers never both replace; the destroy site deletes with no re-insert); single-use consumes — six read-then-assign, none guarded: the magic link (FIXED), the email change (`auth.py::verify_email_change`), both invitation accepts (whose double tap is caught one step later by CK-77.1's backstop on the partial unique), and the two passkey challenges (`passkeys.py::register_complete` and `::signin_complete`) — the five the planning session's. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the tests live in a new file, not `tests/test_race_backstops.py`, whose docstring and the api-reference's convention line pin *seven staged races* for CK-77.1; (2) `WITHDRAWN_MEANWHILE` and its helper existed before the red run, inert with no consumer, because the test file imports the constant and a missing name fails at import rather than on the defect (the CK-76.1 precedent); (3) the table's last-row correction above; (4) the guarded consume runs before the pre-CK-6 refusal, whose return without a commit rolls the stamp back with the session — the row ends exactly as before, `test_pre_ck6_token_cannot_create_account` unedited and green; (5) on a new person the success path's statement count is unchanged (the `add` + `flush` became the one INSERT … RETURNING), and on the link the `refresh` after the commit is gone, the RETURNING carrying the row. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. - **1.83.0** (2026-10-25): Phase CK-77.1 — **the race backstops survive their own rollback** (found reviewing CK-77 and older than it; the second defect reproduced on the deploy on 2026-10-25 at `95e09d9` — two identical first-time `PUT /gatherings/{id}/places/{membership_id}` answered 200 and 500, an asyncpg `UniqueViolationError` on `pk_gathering_member_places` at `api/gatherings.py:1936`; `reference/backend/api-reference.md` 1.40.1 — one convention line and each route's race outcome; **backend only: `api/gatherings.py`, `api/invitations.py`, `api/group_invitations.py`, `api/group_links.py`, `api/rsvps.py`, `services/billing.py` and a new `tests/test_race_backstops.py` — no migration, head stays 0034, the verifier at 361 unchanged, nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, `render.yaml` untouched**; **674 backend tests, 7 new**, no existing test edited; two commits, one per root). **THE TWO DEFECTS, ONE PER RULE.** **(1) After `db.rollback()` every object the session holds is expired — `expire_on_commit=False` governs commits alone — and any read of an expired object on an async session raises `MissingGreenlet`, its id included; `ctx.person` lives in the request's session (FastAPI caches `get_db` per request), so it expires too.** Every backstop that read a loaded object after its rollback answered 500 where its comment promised the idempotent outcome: `accept_invitation` (`row.id`, `row.gathering_id`; since CK-25), `put_rsvp` (`occurrence.id`, `ctx.person.id`; since CK-27), `sync_subscription` (`customer.account_id`; since CK-73), the join approve's re-run `decide()` (`ctx.person.id`; since CK-75). **(2) Autoflush raises the conflict at the NEXT `execute`, wherever the `try` sits**: `put_place` added its row and ran a `delete` before committing, the two admissions added the membership and ran the newcomer helper's `INSERT … SELECT` — the unique violation surfaced THERE, outside a `try` that wrapped only `db.commit()`, and the backstop never ran. Three sites broken twice (`put_place` in both branches, `accept_group_invitation`, the approve), three once. **THE FIXES — each in place, a few lines; no shared wrapper, no retry, no lock, the session factory and `get_auth_context` untouched**: **`put_place` is an upsert with NO backstop** — the place row `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (gathering_id, person_id) DO UPDATE SET place, set_by_person_id, updated_at WHERE the place differs` (an identical place writes nothing, so `updated_at` still means the place changed), the invitation row `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the person-target partial unique (CK-76's helpers' shape) — the race removed rather than caught, `IntegrityError` out of the router's imports; **the two admissions widen the `try`** to enclose the membership add and the helper (their `ON CONFLICT` already covers the place rows) and reload the pending row by an id captured before the write; **the approve** captures the decider's id and the group's before any write and re-runs `decide()` from them; **`put_rsvp`** re-reads by the path's `occurrence_id` and a `person_id` captured before the write; **`sync_subscription`** captures `account_id` before the flush. **What each answers now — the outcome its comment always promised**: `put_place` 200 with the entry as it stands; the two accepts `already_accepted` with the gathering or group id and the token consumed; the approve 200 with the decision's body, the request decided by the approver; `put_rsvp` 200 applied as the update, `updated_at` stamped; the webhook one ERROR line naming both subscription ids and the event id, nothing written, 200. **RED FIRST, EVERY RACE STAGED, NEVER TIMED** (`tests/test_race_backstops.py`, 7): the competing write commits from a SECOND session at one moment of the route's own transaction — `_stage` wraps `AsyncSession.execute` and `AsyncSession.scalar` (`scalar` does not go through `execute`) and fires exactly once, AFTER the route's existence check's SELECT returns (the two accepts, the approve, the RSVP, the webhook) or BEFORE the first DML statement whose autoflush would carry the route's add (`put_place`, both branches); staged at the commit the two would block each other on the unique index and the autoflush defect would never show; each test asserts the hook fired, so none passes vacuously. Against `95e09d9` all seven failed on exactly their defect — `IntegrityError` *raised as a result of Query-invoked autoflush* on `pk_gathering_member_places` (`gatherings.py:1936`, the deploy's own line), `uq_gathering_invitations_gathering_person` (`gatherings.py:1891`) and `uq_memberships_group_id_person_id` (`services/groups.py:342`, from `group_invitations.py:374` and `group_links.py:389`); `MissingGreenlet` at `invitations.py:474`, in `put_rsvp`'s re-read and in `sync_subscription`'s — then green with the code changed and the tests unedited (one assertion of this phase's own dropped before it was ever committed: the webhook's *handled* INFO line is logged on this path as on the pre-check path, and the kickoff asked for no absence of it). **STEP 1, CONFIRMED**: the kickoff's table holds on both axes at all seventeen `rollback()` sites — the safe ones (`add_co_host`, `add_co_admin`, `join_through_link` raise or return constants; the decision's lost race reloads by the path's id; the six media guards raise their 409 or 404 at once) read nothing loaded, and no helper reads `ctx.person` after a rollback beyond the approve's `decide()`. **THE UNBACKSTOPPED UNIQUE WRITES, enumerated and not fixed — the planning session's**: `POST /groups/{id}/link` twice at once (both revoke the live link, both insert, the second violates `uq_group_links_live`); `POST /gatherings/{id}/invitations` and `POST /groups/{id}/invitations` to one destination twice at once (both supersede, both insert, the second violates the live-destination partial unique); `PATCH /media/{id}` with the same tag list twice at once (the second's `DELETE` sees the first's deletions committed and not its inserts, so its own inserts can violate `UNIQUE (media_id, tag)`); `/auth/verify` for two valid links to one new address at once (both find no person, both insert, the second violates `people.email`) — each a 500 today, none in the six handlers. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the staging hook is the route's own statements through `AsyncSession.execute` / `.scalar`, not a monkeypatched loader — it needed no function of the route's to patch and survives the fix that removed `put_place`'s existence check; (2) `put_place` keeps `_holds_invitation` before the `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (an idempotent move still issues no INSERT); (3) the webhook test's *no handled line* assertion, above. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks `(ke)`–`(kg)` are Steven's under the HARNESS RULE's dev-deploy form; the baseline is the 2026-10-25 run on `95e09d9` (one place pair 200 and 500; the RSVP and accept pairs did not collide). Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-77.1-report.md`.
- **1.82.0** (2026-10-24): Phase CK-77 — **Groups 2b: choosing who is invited — shared with, left out, and keeping lets no one in (backend)** (`decisions/2026-10-04-a-groups-gathering-stays-in-the-group.md` 1.3.0 — BUILT banners under §4, §9, §10 and §11; `decisions/2026-09-02-participation-terminology.md` 1.3.0 — §4's memory tier and §5's interim upload rule BUILT; `decisions/2026-09-27-keeping-explained.md` 1.6.0 — §5.11, *keeping lets no one in*, BUILT; `decisions/2026-09-17-keeper-model-v2.md` 1.14.0 — the audience rungs retired, the storage screen the next reader of `keeps_gathering`; `decisions/2026-10-23-joining-a-group-and-co-admins.md` 1.4.0 — §6's newcomer rule and the backup column's drop; `decisions/2026-09-09-who-may-see-an-unapproved-photograph.md` 1.2.0 — the `live` row's audience; `reference/backend/api-reference.md` 1.40.0; `reference/backend/database-schema.md` 2.37.0 — migration 0034, decision 50; `reference/backend/media-pipeline.md` 1.30.0; `reference/architecture/consent-and-compliance.md` 2.6.1; **backend only: `api/gatherings.py`, `api/media.py`, `api/rsvps.py`, `api/groups.py` and `api/profile.py` (comments), `services/groups.py`, `services/keeping.py` (docstrings), `models/enums.py`, `models/gathering.py`, `models/group.py`, `models/__init__.py`, migration 0034, `alembic/env.py` (the hook deleted), `scripts/verify_schema.py` (339 → 361) and tests — nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, `render.yaml` untouched, no new environment variable**; **667 backend tests, 14 new in `tests/test_member_places.py`**, with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **WHAT STEVEN DECIDED (the 2026-10-04 record §4, §11; keeping-explained §5.11, 2026-10-24)**: on a group's gathering each member holds ONE OF THREE PLACES — *invited* (logistics and memory), *shared with* (memory only: the photographs and the gathering's name, never the dates, the RSVP setting, the roster or an upload) and *left out* (no trace on any surface) — the host or a co-host chooses, the rest are shared with automatically, and **keeping lets no one in**: the keeper is charged for every photograph in what they keep and sees nothing through keeping, a gathering in their own group they are left out of included (the surprise party is for them). **THE SHAPE IS A SIBLING RELATION, NEVER A KIND** (migration 0034): `member_place` — `shared_with` | `left_out`, made by `CREATE TYPE` so its labels are usable at once; **invited is not a label** — an invited member holds the person-targeted `gathering_invitations` row the product has always read; `gathering_member_places` (PK `(gathering_id, person_id)`, `place`, `set_by_person_id` nullable — NULL when the group's rule placed a newcomer — `created_at`, `updated_at` stamped when the place CHANGES; three FKs with no delete rule; an index on `person_id`); so every check that admits an invitee stayed correct by construction and **a place row grants nothing until a read path opts in**: `shared_with` is opted into by exactly two — `_gathering_for_memory` and `_visible_media`'s `live` branch — and `left_out` by nothing. `gatherings.invitees_chosen boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` under `ck_gatherings_invitees_chosen_needs_group` (a standalone gathering has no group to choose from) — the server default RIGHT here, unlike `requires_approval`'s, because every writer that does not know about choosing means "the whole group"; **once chosen, chosen** (set true at create by either list and by any move away from invited; nothing sets it back); no backfill. **THE CONTRACT STEP**: `groups.backup_admin_person_id` DROPPED with its FK (retired CK-75, unmapped CK-76 — so the CK-76.1 instance serving during this migration's pre-deploy window selected it nowhere), `alembic/env.py`'s `include_object` hook deleted with it, `alembic check` clean at 0034 with no hook (pinned as a subprocess). **THE TWO READ TIERS** (`api/gatherings.py`): **`_gathering_for_invited`** — RENAMED from `_gathering_for_read`: the host, a co-host, or the caller's person holding an invitation row; taken by the upload intent, RSVP and the publish batch; **`_gathering_for_memory`** — new: the invited tier OR a `shared_with` row, answering which view the caller gets; taken by the detail and the media list; **THE KEEPER TERM IS GONE from both**, from the list's statement (invited OR host OR co-host OR shared with — the kept term of CK-49b retired) and from `_visible_media`'s `live` branch (host OR invitee OR shared with); `keeps_gathering` has NO CALLER and is KEPT ON PURPOSE (its agreement pin keeps the SQL ladder honest; the storage screen is its next reader — a listing of what is charged, never a grant), and an AST pin in `test_member_places.py` fails if any router names it or if any router but `api/media.py` names `resolved_keeper_of`; **the quota path is untouched** — `_enforce_limits` and `account_usage` still resolve the keeper, and a keeper left out of a gathering is charged for its photographs (pinned: the group's keeper left out of a gathering in their own group draws the 404 byte-identical to a missing id on the detail, the media list, an intent and RSVP, finds nothing in either list, and `account_usage` counts the photograph). **`caller_view`** — `"full"` | `"memory"` — on every gathering body (create, detail, list, patch, the group's list); in the memory view `occurrences`, `rsvp_list_visibility`, `next_occurrence` and `occurrence_count` are NULL — withheld, never `[]` or 0 (the CK-30 precedent), and `caller_role` null. **CHOOSING AT CREATE**: `POST /gatherings` with `group_id` takes `invited` and `left_out` — lists of MEMBERSHIP ids (CK-75's one handle), either without `group_id` a 422 at the list; the members in `invited` get the invitation row, those in `left_out` a `left_out` row, **every other live member a `shared_with` row** (`services/groups.py::place_group_members`, three INSERT … SELECT), `invitees_chosen` true; `left_out` alone invites everyone else, `invited: []` invites nobody; each entry a LIVE membership of THIS group (a missing id, another group's and a deleted account one code, `not_a_member`), not the creator's own (`is_host`), listed once across both lists (`duplicate`) — **item-level 422s at `["body", "<list>", N]` carrying `code`**, the batch refused whole, NOTHING written; with neither list the create is CK-76's byte for byte. **THE PLACES ROUTES** — `GET /gatherings/{id}/places` (`{"invitees_chosen", "members": [{"membership_id", "display_name", "place", "co_host"}]}`, one entry per live member but the host, `place` `invited` | `shared_with` | `left_out` | null — a member with no row of either kind, the shape a member who joined after the dates passed holds; ONE statement; never an email, a person id or an account id) and `PUT /gatherings/{id}/places/{membership_id}` (`{"place"}`; to invited writes the invitation row and deletes the place row; to the other two writes or changes the place row with the caller as `set_by`, deletes the invitation row and sets `invitees_chosen`; idempotent), both DELEGABLE (`_gathering_for_organiser` — every invitation act is, the 2026-10-04 record §4), both **409 `not_in_a_group`** on a standalone gathering, the member 404 (CK-75's shape) for an id that is not a live membership of this group, **422 `is_host`** at `["path","membership_id"]`, and two refusals that keep an invited member invited: **409 `is_co_host`** (CK-68's handle is their invitation row; remove them as a co-host first) and **409 `has_answered`** (no answer can be deleted — CK-27; a roster carrying an answer from someone who cannot see the gathering is worse); nothing written on any; nothing logged (pinned at DEBUG — a place list is a roster). **THE NEWCOMER FOLLOWS THE HOST'S CHOICE**: `invite_newcomer_to_upcoming_gatherings` — both admission paths, the same one helper — writes an invitation row on each upcoming gathering whose `invitees_chosen` is false and a `shared_with` row (`set_by` NULL) on each whose flag is true, each excluding gatherings the person holds a row of either kind for, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, idempotent; never the past. **THE UPLOAD AUDIENCE IS THE INVITED TIER** (participation 1.2.0 §5, Steven 2026-10-24): a member only shared with draws the gathering 404 on an intent — they see the photographs and add none; so does the keeper by keeping, who from CK-34 to CK-77 was in the interim audience through the read loader's keeper term. **THE VERIFIER → 0034, 361** (23 new, 1 retired): the table's shape, the PK in key order, three FKs' targets and delete rules, the index, the type's two labels, `invitees_chosen`'s three shape lines and its CHECK by name, and three data lines — no person holds both an invitation row and a place row for one gathering; every place row on a gathering that belongs to a group; no standalone gathering marked chosen — each negative-tested as a subprocess on a plant confirmed by its own SELECT; CK-75's "`backup_admin_person_id` NULL on every row" line REPLACED by the 0009 block's "is gone" line; `gathering_member_places` in the row counts; "every live member holds a place on each upcoming gathering" deliberately NOT asserted — membership moves and rows never do. **STEP 1, CONFIRMED BEFORE WRITING**: `_gathering_for_read` had five callers (the detail, the media list → the memory tier; the intent, the publish batch, RSVP's `_occurrence_for_read` → the invited tier); `keeps_gathering` two (the list's statement, `_visible_media`), both removed; `resolved_keeper_of` two (`_gathering_for_read`, removed; `_enforce_limits`, kept); eleven test files acted as a keeper who is neither host nor invited and expected 200. **The tests** (`tests/test_member_places.py`, 14): the routes behind the auth gate; THE KEEPER LEFT OUT (above) and the sponsorship-shape keeper of a standalone gathering outside its audience; the AST pin; choosing at create — each kind of row with its provenance, the whole-group create unchanged; each create refusal with nothing written; the three places from every surface — the detail's view, the two lists, the media list and a URL, an intent, RSVP, the places list; changing places — every transition's rows, the stamp on a change, `invitees_chosen` only toward true, idempotence; each refused move with nothing written, a co-host changing places, a non-organiser and a standalone gathering; the newcomer by both paths on a whole-group and a chosen gathering; the backup column gone with `alembic check` clean as a subprocess and no `include_object` in `env.py`; the 0034 round trip under the rider with a place row and a chosen flag shown lost and the column back at 0033; the verifier's lines on a clean baseline and each invariant fired by its plant; nothing logged. **The moved pins, each with its reason in the test**: `test_keeper_shape.py`'s two cutover tests INVERTED — `test_a_keeper_who_is_not_the_host_sees_nothing_through_keeping` (with the host NULLed the creator-keeper draws the outsider's 404 everywhere and is charged three photo-equivalents) and `…_shown_to_nobody_through_keeping` (the group's keeper counts a constructed group gathering and sees it nowhere); `test_media_reads.py`'s `_cast` makes the keeper seat honest — its list is the gathering 404, byte-identical to a stranger's — and every live-row loop that read the keeper as admitted now reads the 404 (`test_review.py`, `test_destruction.py`, `test_gathering_bin.py`, `test_removal_attribution.py`, `test_media_words.py`, `test_failure_reason.py` likewise, each stating the seat); `test_co_hosts.py`'s role matrix, its RSVP test on a fresh invitee, its keeper test ending in 404s and its hostless test's former host; `test_gatherings.py`'s switch test (the keeper's read → 404) and its org-backstop test (the caller INVITED as well as keeping — the invitation admits, the column only charges); `test_media_intents.py` — `test_audience_is_the_invited_tier_and_never_keeping` (renamed; the keeper's intent → 404), the hostless test's subject an invitee, the withheld-form test's keeper INVITED to the barbecue so she can upload to it at all; `test_group_gatherings.py` (the admin who keeps a standalone gathering no longer listing it; the backup test → `…_is_unmapped_and_gone`); `test_group_membership.py` (`_backup_admin_column_exists`, the NULL plant gone, the PASS line *is gone*); `test_photo_count.py`'s `HEAD` → 0034; the tallies in `test_co_hosts.py`, `test_destruction.py`, `test_failure_reason.py` and `test_billing.py` 339/338/337/336 → 361/360/359/358, and `test_billing.py`'s two downgraded tallies 261/52 → 260/69 and 264/49 → 263/66 (0034's sixteen lines and the 0009 block's "is gone" line fail below 0034; CK-75's retired line no longer passes), its "alembic_version is at" literals → 0034. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) `_in_invited_tier` admits a co-host by the relation as well as by the invitation row every co-host holds by construction — the criterion's wholeness, the CK-76 list precedent; (2) the create refusals are tested in the order duplicate → not_a_member → is_host, so a duplicated id that is also not a member reads `duplicate` (the kickoff fixed the codes, not the order); (3) a third verifier data line beyond the kickoff's two — no standalone gathering marked chosen, the CHECK re-asserted as data (the 0031 pattern); (4) `decisions/2026-09-08-upload-intent-limits-and-quota.md` (1.3.0) was touched beyond the kickoff's docs list — its §1 stated *host, OR keeper, OR accepted invitee* as the current interim audience (the living-description rule), and carries a dated AMENDED banner; `decisions/2026-09-03-co-hosts.md` §5's *stay as they are* on the backup column was left, its 1.5.0 banner already recording the slot's retirement; (5) **a residue recorded, not fixed**: a member who uploaded while invited and was then moved to shared with or left out keeps the UPLOADER's own rights on those rows by media id — their own pending or removed rows through `_visible_media`'s uploader branches, confirm, the caption, the bin acts and dismiss — because the uploader rule is the row's and closing it would change CK-37's audience, which the kickoff fenced; homed for the planning session. - **1.81.0** (2026-10-23): Phase CK-76.1 — **the account-deleted screen shows** (CK-76's observation 1 — the check runs of 2026-10-23: two throwaway accounts deleted through the Settings form, six samples from 1.2 s to 7.5 s after confirming, every one the sign-in page at `/`, CK-8's *Your account is deleted* never rendered; the planning session's kickoff, agreed by Steven 2026-10-23; `reference/frontend/components.md` 1.29.0; **frontend only: `routes/Settings.tsx`, `routes/AccountDeleted.tsx`, a new `lib/accountDeleted.ts` and a new `routes/account-deleted.test.tsx`; `components/RequireAuth.tsx`, `auth/` and `lib/session.ts` untouched; nothing under `backend/`, no migration, neither lock touched, `render.yaml`, `netlify.toml`, `vite.config.ts` and `frontend/.nvmrc` untouched**; **242 frontend tests, 7 new** — the 235 existing green and unedited, `routes/settings.test.tsx`'s three among them; build + lint clean; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT, PROVED RED BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED**: STEP 1 rendered the app's route table — the real `RequireAuth` around `/settings` — with the real `AuthProvider` over the real session store, signed in, typed `DELETE`, pressed the control against a 204, and read **`{ location: '/', heading: 'CoveyKeep', signInForm: true }` with the auth state `signedOut`** where the fix reads `/account-deleted` and the heading. The mechanism, from the installed router's source: `Settings.tsx::handleDelete` called `navigate('/account-deleted', { replace: true })` and then `clearToken()`, its comment promising one batched render; React Router 7's `MemoryRouter` and `BrowserRouter` both wrap their location update in `React.startTransition` (unless `useTransitions === false`), so the navigation was a TRANSITION, while `clearToken()` notified `lib/session.ts`'s listeners and `AuthProvider`'s subscriber set the auth state to signed-out as an URGENT update — React rendered the urgent update first, with the route still `/settings`, `RequireAuth` saw signed-out and rendered `<Navigate to="/" replace />`, and that navigation superseded the pending one. **Corrected from the kickoff's history**: the comment was not true of an earlier router — the lockfile has carried `react-router` 7.18.2 and React 19.2.8 unchanged since CK-6 (`e4e5e49`, CK-8's own commit, resolved the same versions as today), so the batching the comment promised never happened, and nothing drove the deletion through the real route table with `RequireAuth` until this phase (`routes/settings.test.tsx` renders `Settings` alone under a fake context; CK-19's doc audit corrected the components doc to the code's order, still believing the batching). **THE FIX MAKES THE ORDER STRUCTURAL — the kickoff's recommended shape; nothing simpler held**: `handleDelete` ONLY navigates, with the one-shot signal **`ACCOUNT_DELETED_SIGNAL`** — `{ accountDeleted: true }`, `lib/accountDeleted.ts`, with `carriesAccountDeletedSignal(state)` beside it — as the navigation's state, and its comment says what is true; `AccountDeleted` reads `location.state` and, in a mount effect that runs ONLY when the signal is present, calls `clearToken()` and then `navigate(location.pathname, { replace: true, state: null })`: the signed-out render lands on an unguarded route, so nothing bounces, and the replace spends the signal, so a reload, Back or a later visit finds none. **A direct visit to `/account-deleted` — typed, bookmarked, signed in or out — shows the screen and signs nobody out** (pinned: the token still stored and the auth state `signedIn` after the render settles; signed out, the screen and nothing thrown). **THE WINDOW IS EMPTY**: between the 204 and the clear the token is still stored while the server has anonymized the person, so any `authFetch` would 401 and `lib/api.ts` would clear the token at `/settings` — the same bounce; the test's server answers 401 to every bearer request after the deletion and the request log holds NOTHING after `POST /me/delete` (`Settings` fetches at mount — `/billing`, `/me/passkeys` — and on the person's own acts, and the delete is the last request the page makes), so the 401 path does not arise on this flow; a request already in flight at the click is bounded by those mount-time reads, long resolved by the time anyone has typed `DELETE`, and is recorded as the residue rather than fixed — closing it would need `RequireAuth` to change, which the kickoff forbade. `RequireAuth` untouched; no delay, no timer; the copy CK-8's word for word; the request and the confirmation typing unchanged. **The tests** (`routes/account-deleted.test.tsx`, 7): the deletion through the route table — red first — landing on `/account-deleted` with the heading, the two lines and *Back to sign in* → `/`, the session gone only there and the screen staying put, the path never through `/`, the `/account-deleted` location seen first with exactly `{ accountDeleted: true }` and last with null, no request after the delete and none ever sent without its token; the direct visit signed in (the token kept, `signedIn`, no navigation); signed out (the screen, nothing thrown); the copy as literals (one `h1`, exactly two paragraphs, one link); a failed deletion (a 500) reading *Something went wrong deleting your account. - **1.78.0** (2026-10-23): Phase CK-75 — **Groups 1: joining a group, the member list, co-admins, and handing over (backend)** (`decisions/2026-10-23-joining-a-group-and-co-admins.md` 1.1.0 — BUILT banners under §2, §3, §5, §6, §7 and §8; `decisions/2026-09-14-groups-get-a-surface.md` 1.1.1 — its two `SUPERSEDED IN DESIGN` banners converted to `SUPERSEDED`; `reference/backend/api-reference.md` 1.38.0 — the Groups router rewritten whole with the classification one line per check; `reference/backend/database-schema.md` 2.35.0 — migration 0032 and decision 48; **backend only: `models/group.py` and `models/enums.py`, migration 0032, `api/groups.py` rewritten, NEW `api/group_links.py` and `api/group_invitations.py`, `security.py`, `services/groups.py`, `services/retention.py`, `api/profile.py`'s call, `main.py`, `scripts/verify_schema.py` (271 → 336) and tests; nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, `render.yaml` untouched, no new environment variable**; **640 backend tests, 34 new** — `tests/test_group_membership.py`, `tests/test_group_links.py`, `tests/test_group_invitations.py` — with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **WHAT STEVEN DECIDED (record §1, 2026-10-22/23)**: a group is joined through ONE link the family shares from the phone — CoveyKeep sends nothing, so A2P does not apply and no phone number is stored — and **every join through it waits for the admin's or a co-admin's yes**, because a link in a family text gets forwarded; beside it an emailed invitation per person admits directly (the CK-25 machinery, the group as its target; `SMS` still refused). Any member sees and shares the link; the admin and co-admins hold the door. Every member sees the member list — display names, never an email, a phone number or an account id. Co-admins are a relation beside the one admin, on the PERSON spine, the co-host shape; reserved to the admin: appointing and removing co-admins, handing over, archiving and the history setting (both unbuilt); delegable: approving and declining joins, emailed invitations, the link, removing ordinary members, renaming. A member leaves at will; the admin hands over first, and an admin who is the only member just leaves. On account deletion the longest-serving co-admin becomes the admin. **Running a group and keeping it never touch.** `POST /groups` had never written the keeper — every group since 0022 had none — and is fixed with a one-time backfill. **MIGRATION 0032**: four new tables and one new type, nothing dropped or renamed on a live table — `group_co_admins` (PK `(group_id, person_id)`, `added_by_person_id` as provenance, an index on `person_id`, no delete rule, no capability column ever), `group_invitations_pending` (0011's columns with `group_id`, on the EXISTING `invitation_channel` type whose labels the migration never names — a SIBLING table, never a widened target; the same CHECK and live-destination partial unique under their own names), `group_links` (`id`, `group_id`, `created_by_person_id`, `created_at`, `expires_at`, `revoked_at` — **NO TOKEN COLUMN**; the partial unique `uq_group_links_live` on `(group_id) WHERE revoked_at IS NULL`), `group_join_requests` (`decided_at`, `outcome` on the NEW `join_request_outcome` type — `approved` | `declined`, made by `CREATE TYPE` — `decided_by_person_id` nullable; the CHECK `decided_with_outcome`; the partial unique `uq_group_join_requests_open` on `(group_id, person_id) WHERE decided_at IS NULL`); then **the keeper backfill, last**: `groups.keeper_account_id` = the admin person's account only where the keeper is NULL, the admin is set and the admin's person is not anonymized, the count on an ASCII INFO line on alembic's own logger (zero is a finding on the deploy as much as any number; locally it read zero — no groups). The downgrade drops the four tables and the type; the backfilled keepers STAY (they are true; 0022 wrote the same fact). Up/down/up pinned against four planted subjects with the INFO line verbatim. **`groups.backup_admin_person_id` leaves every body and is written by nothing — and is NOT dropped**: the CK-74.2 instance still serving during the pre-deploy migration selects it in every group body, so the drop is a later contract step; the verifier asserts it NULL on every row. **THE TOKEN IS NEVER STORED**: `<link id>.<MAC>`, the MAC an HMAC-SHA256 under the session secret over a context string of its own plus the link id, base64url without padding (`security.py::group_link_token` / `parse_group_link_token`, constant-time; an unknown id and a wrong MAC are the same `invalid`); any member can be shown the link again, a leaked database yields no working link, and rotating the session secret kills every link as it kills every session — accepted. **Never logged, on any path** (pinned with the root at DEBUG, the success paths included). The URL is `${APP_BASE_URL}/groups/join#token=…` — the fragment, never a query string; the frontend route does not exist yet (Groups 3), so it lands on the app's not-found screen until then — the CK-73 `/plan` precedent, stated in the router's docstring. **THE HELPER PAIR** (`api/groups.py`, the CK-68 shape): `is_group_admin` — the RESERVED set — and `may_administer_group` — the admin or a co-admin — with SQL forms `co_administers_group` / `administers_group` / `member_of`; the loaders `_group_for_read` (a member or the admin), `_group_for_organiser` (delegable) and `_group_for_admin` (reserved); **every group check classified as one or the other on purpose**, one line per check in the api-reference. **THE ROUTES**: `GET /groups/{id}/members` (any member: `membership_id` — the one handle, the CK-68 idea — `display_name`, `role`, `is_self`; the anonymized absent; one statement); `POST /groups/{id}/co-admins` (the admin; `membership_id`; 422 codes `not_a_member` / `is_admin` / `already_co_admin` / `account_deleted`, no row on any) and `DELETE …/co-admins/{membership_id}` (the admin removes anyone, idempotently; a co-admin steps down; a co-admin naming another is the group's 404); `POST /groups/{id}/leave` (the admin with another live member refused with a 409 `hand_over_first`; the only member leaves and the admin goes NULL); `DELETE /groups/{id}/members/{membership_id}` (the admin anyone but themselves; a co-admin an ordinary member only; the caller's own membership the leave route's — the member 404); `POST /groups/{id}/hand-over` (the admin; immediate; the former admin an ordinary member; the new admin's co-admin row deleted; 422 codes `not_a_member` / `is_admin` / `account_deleted`); `PATCH /groups/{id}` now delegable; `POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /groups/{id}/link` (organisers make, replace and revoke; any member reads `{"url", "expires_at"}` or nulls); `POST /group-links/preview` (unauthenticated — `valid` | `expired` | `revoked` | `invalid`, the group's name on valid) and `POST /group-links/join` (`already_member` with the group id — the only outcome that carries it — `pending`, `requested`; a dead token its status; 429 past 50 requests per group per `RATE_WINDOW`, decided rows counting; two concurrent joins make ONE open request); `GET /groups/{id}/join-requests` and `…/{request_id}/approve` | `/decline` (organisers; one guarded UPDATE keyed on `decided_at IS NULL`, approve writing the membership in the same transaction; 409 `already_decided`); `POST`/`GET /groups/{id}/invitations`, `DELETE …/invitations/{id}`, `POST /group-invitations/preview` and `/accept` — CK-25 exactly (the constants `MAX_INVITATIONS_PER_GROUP` / `MAX_GROUP_INVITATIONS_PER_INVITER` bound to CK-25's values; the per-inviter tally counts group rows alone — two tables, two tallies, recorded), the acceptance creating the membership and closing an open request as `approved` by nobody; **every body carries `caller_role`** — `admin` | `co_admin` | `member` — and `member_count` counts the people the list shows. **Whether a declined requester is told is OPEN** (record §12): following the link again simply makes a new request, in the docstring. **THE DELETION LEG** — `services/groups.py::lapse_group_roles`, RENAMED from `relinquish_group_admin` for what it now does: for each group the person administers the longest-serving co-admin (earliest `created_at`, then `person_id`) becomes the admin and their row is deleted, or the admin goes NULL with none; the person's co-admin rows and OPEN join requests are deleted; the backup NULLing stays; memberships RETAINED; `updated_at` unstamped. **THE KEEPER NEVER MOVES** on a hand-over, an appointment, a leave or a removal — pinned on each; a keeper who leaves still keeps, a group can be kept by an outsider, keeping moves only by the unbuilt keeping flows. **THE REAPER**: `EPHEMERAL_TABLES` at seven — invitations and links by `expires_at`, join requests by `decided_at` (an open request has none and is never reaped by age); `PURGE_GRACE > RATE_WINDOW` holds for every tally. **THE VERIFIER → 0032, 336** (65 new; one REPLACED): the four tables' shapes, the ten FKs with no delete rule, the index, the four partial/unique indexes by name with their predicates, the two absent token columns, the new type's labels, the two CHECKs, the group invitation's normalization line, and five invariants — **every group whose admin is set has a membership for that admin** (REPLACING CK-45's *every group has at least one membership*, which an only member leaving now legitimately breaks; the new line still catches the CK-45 failure), every co-admin holds a membership, no co-admin is the admin, no co-admin row and no open request names an anonymized person, `backup_admin_person_id` NULL on every row — each negative-tested as a subprocess on a plant confirmed by its own SELECT. **The moved pins, each with its reason in the test**: `test_groups.py`'s body key set (`backup_admin_person_id` out, `caller_role` in), its create pin, and its rename test — RENAMED `test_rename_is_refused_to_an_ordinary_member_with_the_group_404` (renaming is delegable now; an ordinary member is still refused); `test_account_deletion.py`'s group test (the leg's new name; the keeper asserted written by the router rather than set by the test); `test_retention.py`'s `EPHEMERAL_TABLES` pin at seven; `test_photo_count.py`'s `HEAD` → 0032; the tallies in `test_co_hosts.py`, `test_destruction.py`, `test_failure_reason.py` and `test_billing.py` 271/270/269/268 → 336/335/334/333, and `test_billing.py`'s two downgrade tallies (0030: 259/12 → 259/51; 0031: 262/9 → 262/48 — the four group tables absent at those heads, every line reading one of them failing and the per-column lines not emitted), their comments extended. **STEP 15, THE NOTICE** (read against what this phase stores; not edited): no sentence of version 2 becomes false — *the gatherings and groups you create or join* covers a membership, a co-admin row and a join request (a request names your own person, ephemeral once decided); *the email addresses of people you invite, which are cleared after the invitation runs out a week later* covers the group invitation exactly (the same seven days, the same reaper); the link stores no token and no phone number; the console-mode disclosure already names invitations. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the kickoff's `GET /groups/{id}/link` body for no link is `{"url": null}` — built as `{"url": null, "expires_at": null}`, one shape either way; (2) the kickoff names no response for approve/decline — built as `{"id", "display_name", "created_at", "outcome", "decided_at"}`; (3) the member route's refusals for the caller's own membership and for a co-admin naming the admin or another co-admin are the member 404 (hidden, the co-host precedent) — the kickoff fixed the rule and not the shape; (4) `GET /groups/{id}/invitations` carries `pending` alone, no `accepted` list — an accepted invitee is a member, on the member list; (5) the per-inviter invitation tally counts group rows alone, so twenty gathering and twenty group invitations fit one window; (6) the verifier adds sixty-five lines, not the kickoff's unnumbered list — the token-hash unique and the two absent token columns among them; (7) `_group_for_organiser` is the delegable loader's name (the CK-68 idiom) where the kickoff said `may_administer_group` alone. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks `(jl)`–`(jp)` are Steven's, on fresh `@example.com` throwaways and a fresh test group, never his household group for any write. - **1.77.0** (2026-10-22): Phase CK-74.2 — **Checkout hides Link, and offers cards only on purpose** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md` 1.9.0 — the second AMENDED banner in §11, directly after CK-74.1's, §13 item 7's clause, DATA-HANDLING's dated parenthesis, the Status line; `reference/backend/api-reference.md` 1.37.2; **backend only: `services/billing.py::checkout_url` and `tests/test_billing.py` — NO migration, head stays 0031, the verifier at 271 unchanged, neither lock touched, nothing under `frontend/`; the portal session, `GET /billing`, the webhook, the quota path and the worker untouched**; **606 backend tests, the total unchanged** — the one test renamed again, red first; two commits, one per root). **WHAT `(jh)` FOUND (2026-10-22), and what the 1.76.0 entry below got wrong**: CK-74.1 sent `allowed_payment_method_types: ["card"]` to turn Stripe Link off, and its entry says Link is OFF — on the deploy it was not. The filter WORKED as a filter: the methods went from *Card / Cash App Pay / Klarna / Bank* to *Card / Bank* and the wallet row from *Apple Pay / Link / Amazon Pay* to *Apple Pay / Link*, so Cash App Pay, Klarna and Amazon Pay were gone — and Link stayed whole: the pre-ticked *Save my information with Link for faster checkout* box, the phone-number field beneath it, *By paying, you agree to Link's Terms and Privacy.* and the Link button in the wallet row (the remaining *Bank* probably Link's own bank payment; `(jk)` notes whether it is still offered). **Checkout's Link is not a payment method the filter governs** (the Gotcha below). **STEP 1, THE PROBE, before anything was written** (the installed `stripe` 16.0.0, API `2026-09-30.endive`; the kickoff's three facts, every one held): `SessionCreateParams` carries `wallet_options`, typed `SessionCreateParamsWalletOptions`, whose one key is `link`, typed `SessionCreateParamsWalletOptionsLink`, whose one key is `display`, with the literals `auto` and `never` (and the SDK's `|str` escape hatch beside them) and the docstring *Specifies whether Checkout should display Link as a payment option. By default, Checkout will display all the supported wallets that the Checkout Session was created with. This is the `auto` behavior, and it is the default choice.* **THE BACKEND**: `checkout_url` adds **`"wallet_options": {"link": {"display": "never"}}`** beside the filter, which stays; every other parameter unchanged; the docstring's CK-74.1 paragraph rewritten to say what is true — **the card-only filter is Steven's decision of 2026-10-22, standing on its own and no longer a side effect** (cards, with Apple Pay and Google Pay riding them, cover nearly everyone; the list is fixed in code, so sandbox and live match; and for a $4.99 monthly plan, bank payments and buy-now-pay-later add ways for a payment to fail without adding buyers), Link is hidden by `wallet_options` and not by the filter, Link stays off by Steven's decision of 2026-10-21 for the reason the paragraph already gave (a pre-ticked box is not a choice the product made), the `excluded_` reasoning kept (it still cannot name `link`), and **a parameter's documentation does not prove what Checkout shows — only a look at the sandbox does, which is why `(jk)` exists**. **The test**: the exact-dict pin gains `wallet_options` with its reason, asserts `display` is `never`, keeps the `"link"`-absent assertion on the allowed list, and is RENAMED `…_the_two_urls_card_only_with_link_hidden_and_nothing_else` — the dict is still exact, so *and nothing else* still holds. **Red first**: the renamed test against the unchanged `checkout_url` failed on the exact dict, the key absent from what the seam received; then green. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) prerequisite 1 was NOT met — the Hub's `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` was dirty at 1.184.0 (the planning session's own entry, which wrote this kickoff) when the phase began; the CK-59/60/61/66/68/73.1 precedent applied — the file untouched, the docs commit staging its two files by path, the breach heads the report; (2) the kickoff's step 2 says the filter removed "the plain bank option", but its own CONTEXT and `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` 1.184.0 record *Bank* REMAINING (*Card / Bank*), probably Link's own — the docstring and every doc say what `(jh)` found, and `(jk)` notes whether *Bank* is still offered once Link is hidden; (3) the record's Status line gains a CK-74.2 clause beyond the kickoff's list — it named CK-74.1's correction as the latest, and a living description must not (the CK-62/CK-65/CK-66 precedent); (4) the DATA-HANDLING parenthesis is plain text with a bold dated lead rather than an italic aside, so the correction renders no quieter than the clause it corrects (the supersession form's rule, applied to the shape the kickoff asked for). Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks `(jj)`–`(jk)` are Steven's, sandbox and test cards only, on a fresh `@example.com` throwaway; **`(jk)` decides: if the Link box or button is still there, the fallback is the Dashboard's Link switch, set in sandbox and live alike, and that is Steven's call.** Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-74.2-report.md`.

Payment is handled by Stripe, so your card never reaches CoveyKeep.* — both from `brand.ts`. **A Checkout or portal URL never leaves its call** (the presigned-URL rule): `startCheckout` and `openPortal` read it from the response, hand it to `follow` — `window.location.assign` in the app, a spy in a test, a prop on `Plan` for tests only — and answer with an outcome carrying no URL; pinned in exactly one place and nowhere in the DOM or any other request. **Refusals from the code, never the wording**: 409 `already_subscribed` → *You already have a plan.
