- **1.74.0** (2026-10-20): Phase CK-73.4 — **the notice's version 2 says what can be deleted and what the log holds, corrected before anyone accepts it** (`decisions/2026-08-31-private-alpha-scope.md` 1.4.0 — §3.1's version 2 block and an AMENDED banner above it; the two clauses CK-73.3's report flagged in its §0, both confirmed against the code by the planning session on 2026-10-20 and both wordings Steven's verbatim; **frontend only: `routes/Tos.tsx` and `routes/tos.test.tsx` — no backend file, no migration, head stays 0031, `TOS_VERSION` stays 2 on both sides, the validator untouched, neither lock touched**; **179 frontend tests, the total unchanged** — every new pin landed inside the three existing content tests, `routes/tos.test.tsx` 6 → 6; the 602 backend tests not re-run, nothing under `backend/` in the diff; build + lint clean; two commits, one per root, each on top of CK-73.3's, **and the two phases are pushed together** — the version 2 anyone accepts is the corrected one, so no version 3 is needed). **THE TWO SENTENCES, AND NOTHING ELSE** (every other paragraph, the heading and the `Link` byte-identical — the read-back below): **(a)** *"Delete them first if you'd rather they went"* → *"Delete your photos first if you'd rather they went. Answers can't be deleted, but you can change yours to "Can't make it" and remove anyone you said you were bringing."* — "them" was photos AND answers, and no answer can be deleted: `api/rsvps.py` has a `@router.put` and a `@router.get` and no delete, deliberately (CK-27 — changing your mind is `response: "no"`), so after account deletion the roster still shows *a former member — Can't make it*; the new sentence was checked against the code before it was written — *Can't make it* is the `no` label in `lib/rsvps.ts`, a "no" with companions is refused and `companionsToSend` sends an empty list on a "no", so `_replace_companions` clears them (CK-29's clearing path), and every companion row has its own remove control; **(b)** *"sign-in links are printed to a server log, and Steven — who runs the server — can see them"* → *"sign-in links, invitations and email-change confirmations are printed to a server log, with the email address each one is for, and Steven — who runs the server — can see them"* — `services/email.py`'s one console-mode line, `[email] console-mode send ok to={to} …`, prints EVERY email the app sends with its address, and the four send sites are the sign-in link (`api/auth.py`), the invitation (`api/invitations.py` — a third party's address, supplied by the inviter) and both email-change messages (`api/profile.py`: the confirmation to the new address, the notice to the old one); the paragraph's second sentence unchanged. **WHY UNDER THE SAME NUMBER**: the record's §3.1 rule is that a version's text never changes after accounts have accepted it, and none has — nothing is pushed (Steven, 2026-10-20); the Gotcha's rule (2) names exactly this case. **Tests** (`routes/tos.test.tsx`; **red first** — the moved and new pins run against CK-73.3's `Tos.tsx`: 3 failed, 3 passed, each failure on exactly its pin; then green): the server-log pin MOVED with its reason — `/sign-in links are printed to a server log/i` no longer matches because the list of what is printed grew — replaced by the fuller sentence and `/with the email address each one is for/i`; NEW `/Delete your photos first if you'd rather they went/i` and `/Answers can't be deleted, but you can change yours to [“"]Can't make it[”"]/i` (either quote style, the *a former member* pin's form); the negation test gains `/Delete them first/i` over the whole rendered page and is retitled for both versions' false sentences; every other pin unchanged and green. **Rendered and compared to §3.1 paragraph by paragraph: 9 of 9 identical** (the throwaway read-back, never committed). **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) one pin ADDED beyond its list — the `no` label the sentence quotes is read from `RSVP_RESPONSES` in `lib/rsvps.ts` and asserted present on the page, so a relabelled button fails the notice's test instead of leaving it naming a choice nobody can find — this phase's lesson made a test; (2) `Tos.tsx`'s header comment gains one paragraph saying why the text changed under its number — rendered text untouched beyond the two sentences, which the read-back proves; (3) the kickoff's "179 plus the new" presumed new test functions; the pins live inside the existing tests, so the total is 179; (4) `reference/frontend/components.md` quotes neither sentence (its `/tos` row names *the console-mode log disclosure* as a topic) and is untouched, as the kickoff allowed. Not pushed — Steven pushes CK-73.3 and CK-73.4 together after review; CK-73.3's checks `(ja)`–`(jb)` run once after the joint push, `(ja)` reading both new sentences, no new check letter. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-73.4-report.md`.
- **1.73.0** (2026-10-19): Phase CK-73.3 — **the private test notice, version 2: true about what is stored** (`decisions/2026-08-31-private-alpha-scope.md` 1.3.0 — §3 as amended by Steven 2026-10-19, §3.1 holding both texts word for word; the design session's finding of 2026-10-14 and the planning session's audit of 2026-10-19; **frontend `routes/Tos.tsx`, `lib/api.ts`, `routes/tos.test.tsx`; backend `api/auth.py`'s constant and `tests/test_auth.py` — no migration, head stays 0031, the verifier at 271 unchanged, neither lock touched, the worker and billing untouched**; **179 frontend tests** (177 + 2 — `routes/tos.test.tsx` 4 → 6), **602 backend tests** (600 + 2 — one test became three), with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT**: `/tos` — the text the sign-in checkbox records agreement to, by number alone — said *"Photo upload doesn't exist yet, so no photos are stored"* from CK-36 (2026-09-09), when the first photograph was stored, until the design session found it on 2026-10-14; it also said *a development database* where photos live in a bucket and a plan at Stripe, and named none of: photos with their caption, tags and file name (CK-36–CK-40), RSVP answers and companions' names (CK-27/29), groups (CK-45), the addresses of people you invite (CK-25), a passkey (CK-10), what deletion leaves behind (anonymization — photos and answers stay under `ANONYMIZED_DISPLAY_NAME`, *a former member*; the omission that mattered most for consent), or test-mode plans (CK-73/74). **`routes/tos.test.tsx` PINNED the false sentence in place** — right at CK-26, and the reason it stayed: a pin on a claim about what is not built passes until the thing is built and goes on passing, so nine data-storing phases ran green past it and none opened the page. The project's recurring defect class, on its consent surface (the Gotcha below). **THE TEXT IS STEVEN'S, VERBATIM** (approved 2026-10-19; §3.1 — the one clause corrected after approval and reported to him: accepting an invitation does not clear the address, the pending row's week does): the opening, server-log, terms and closing paragraphs are version 1's byte for byte; *Everything is stored on test systems*, and a photo named as the thing to keep a copy of; the what-is-stored paragraph — identity, *the gatherings and groups you create or join*, answers *including the names of anyone you say you're bringing*, *the email addresses of people you invite, which are cleared after the invitation runs out a week later*, sign-in records, *a passkey if you add one*; a photographs paragraph — seen by the gathering with caption, tags and file name, *three copies of each photo at different sizes and deletes the file you uploaded*, *location and other camera details are removed*, your own deletable, *a photo sent to a bin is deleted for good 30 days later* (true only since `(gt)` switched the sweep on, 2026-10-19 — which is why that check was this phase's prerequisite), *Deleting your account removes your email address and name, but photos and answers you added stay in their gatherings under "a former member"*; a plans paragraph — *Stripe's test mode: no real payment is taken, and a real card won't work … never your card*. **Rendered and compared to §3.1 paragraph by paragraph: identical.** **Each sentence checked against the code before it was written**: `profile.py` (email NULL, `ANONYMIZED_DISPLAY_NAME`, every contribution kept), `invitations.py` (`INVITATION_TTL` seven days; `consumed_at` the only stamp at accept; reaped by `expires_at`; the accepted row carries a person id, never the address), `processing.py` (three layers, nothing but pixels to the encoder), `worker.py::_delete_original` after the `ready` commit, `destruction.py` (`REMOVED_BIN` 30 days), `config.py` (`rk_test_`). **THE VERSION**: `TOS_VERSION` → **2** on both sides, each with a comment saying what 1 and 2 are and that the published terms take **3** — the record's rule: an acceptance is recorded at account creation against the number alone (`api/auth.py::verify`), so one number must mean one text and a rewrite under 1 would make 1 mean two; **the validator stays `1 <= value <= TOS_VERSION`** — a browser still on the old bundle (the PWA keeps it until its update notice is taken) showed its user version 1's text, so 1 is the TRUE record for that sign-up, never a stale one; no migration, no backfill, every existing row stays 1 (every account is Steven's household, confirmed 2026-10-19; nobody is re-asked, and re-acceptance is queue item 0b's, for the published terms). **Tests**: `routes/tos.test.tsx` 4 → 6 — the *Photo upload doesn't exist* pin REPLACED BY ITS NEGATION over the whole rendered page (*no photos are stored* with it), the what-is-collected pin following the new wording, a second content test pinning three copies and the deleted original / location removed / your own deletable / 30 days / *a former member* / a week / test mode and never your card, `TOS_VERSION === 1` → `=== 2` with its comment rewritten (1, 2, 3; never renumber), the brand-source pin and the checkbox test unchanged — **the three content tests failed first against the old `Tos.tsx`** (swapped in from HEAD for one run, restored byte-identical); `tests/test_auth.py` — `_request_link` sends `auth_api.TOS_VERSION` (the helper models the current bundle), the records-the-version test rewritten without its monkeypatch as *version 2 records 2*, NEW *version 1 still records 1 — the stale-bundle case* and *3 is refused, the published terms are not published*, the unknown-version test given its reason. **The moved pins, each with its reason in the test**: the happy path's `tos[0].version == 1` → `== auth_api.TOS_VERSION` (the row records what the form sent); the `TOS_VERSION === 1` guard. **ONE CLAUSE FLAGGED, NOT CHANGED** (the kickoff's rule — no rewording): *"Delete them first if you'd rather they went"* is fully actionable for photos and only partly for answers — an RSVP can be changed to *Can't make it* and its companions removed, but no answer can be deleted (CK-27 shipped no delete endpoint, deliberately), so an anonymized person's answer row stays in the roster under *a former member*; **until the push no account has accepted version 2, so its text may still change under 2; after the first acceptance a change is version 3** — Steven's call before `(ja)`. Also noted: in console mode the invitation email, destination included, prints to the same server log the notice discloses for sign-in links. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the baseline was 600 backend tests, not 585 — CK-73.2 had landed; (2) the record's Status line and §1 condition 4 still stated *version 1 / published terms = version 2* as current fact and are amended beside the BUILT banner (the living-description rule); (3) the happy-path pin above, which the kickoff's list did not name; (4) this file's Commands `npm run test` comment gains the notice's line. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks `(ja)`–`(jb)` are Steven's; `(jb)`'s throwaway account records 2 and every earlier row still reads 1. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-73.3-report.md`.
- **1.72.0** (2026-10-19): Phase CK-73.2 — **a downgrade waiting for renewal is stored and shown** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md` 1.6.0 — §12 as amended 2026-10-19, §2's portal bullet, §7; the design session's finding of 2026-10-14: the plan screen could not say *switches to 100,000 photos on Nov 12*; **backend only: `models/billing.py`, migration 0031, `services/billing.py`, `api/billing.py`, `scripts/verify_schema.py` (261 → 271) and tests — plus `render.yaml`, the rider alone; nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, the worker and `config.py` untouched**; **600 backend tests**, 15 new in `tests/test_billing.py` (ten functions, one parametrised over the six events), with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **WHAT HAPPENS AT STRIPE**: the Customer Portal downgrades at the end of the period (§7) with a SUBSCRIPTION SCHEDULE — the subscription's `schedule` names a `sub_sched_…` with two phases, the current price until the period ends and the lower price after — and CoveyKeep read the current item's price alone (§2), so the plan and the allowance stayed right until renewal, by design, and nothing knew a change was coming. **STEP 1, THE PROBE against the installed `stripe` 16.0.0, before anything was written**: `Subscription.schedule` is `str | SubscriptionSchedule | None` — an id string unexpanded, None with no schedule; `SubscriptionSchedule.phases[].items[].price` is `str | Price` — an id unexpanded; `SubscriptionRetrieveParams` carries `expand: List[str]`, and `retrieve(id, params={"expand": ["schedule.phases.items.price"]})` builds — four levels, Stripe's limit; `construct_from` with the nested schedule yields `SubscriptionSchedule`, `Phase`, `Item`, `Price` and `CurrentPhase` objects, every one subscript-readable and every one refusing `.get` (the CK-73 Gotcha); `SubscriptionSchedule.status` is `active | canceled | completed | not_started | released`, `.subscription` `str | Subscription | None`, `.released_subscription` `Optional[str]`. **THE SMALLEST FORM CHOSEN: ONE retrieve with that expansion** (`SUBSCRIPTION_EXPAND`, inside `_retrieve_subscription` — the one wrapper beneath the functions the routers call, so tests stub beneath the decision; pinned on the wrapper and on the SDK's own `expand` parameter), never a second fetch of the schedule by id. **MIGRATION 0031**: `subscriptions.scheduled_plan` (`billing_plan`, NULL) and `subscriptions.scheduled_plan_at` (timestamptz, NULL), no default, nothing backfilled (a migration cannot ask Stripe; the next event writes them, and on the deploy check (ix) makes one), under the CHECK **`ck_subscriptions_scheduled_plan_both_or_neither`** — `(scheduled_plan IS NULL) = (scheduled_plan_at IS NULL)`: a plan and an instant are one fact, and half of one is a row nothing wrote; the existing type is named and none of its labels (the CK-71 trap has nothing to bite on); invisible to the old instance under the pre-deploy ordering; the downgrade drops the CHECK and both columns; up/down/up pinned with a planted pair shown lost and the CHECK refused on a real row by name, both halves. **`sync_subscription` WRITES THEM** (`_scheduled_change`, everything through `_field`): when the schedule's status is `active` or `not_started`, the first phase whose `start_date` is at or after the current phase's `end_date` — or, with no current phase (the `not_started` shape), the item's period end — is the next phase; its one item's price names the plan by lookup key; that plan and that phase's start are written, **null both** for no schedule, no such phase, the same plan, or a schedule in any other status; **a next phase that cannot be read as a configured plan — an unknown lookup key, a price or a schedule that came back as an id — is ONE ERROR line naming the event and subscription ids (the tenth line under the CK-73.1 enumeration), both columns null, and the rest of the upsert written as usual**: it never blocks the current plan. **THE ALLOWANCE NEVER READS THEM** — pinned behaviourally (300,000 and `holds_paid_plan` True with a 100k change scheduled; 100,000 once the schedule completes and the item moves) and structurally (a token scan of `keeping.py` and `api/media.py` for `scheduled`; the body's figure from `keeping.PLAN_PHOTOGRAPHS`, never a column; `next_allowance` and `scheduled_allowance` asserted absent by the verifier). **`GET /billing` CARRIES `scheduled_change`** — `{"plan", "allowance", "at"}` or null; **null whenever `ends_at` is set** (a plan that ends has no next plan, and Stripe releases the schedule on cancel anyway — CK-73.1's finding; should both arrive together the ending wins on the surface and the row still holds what Stripe holds, pinned in both cancellation shapes); every other key unchanged. **THE WEBHOOK LEARNS THE SIX SCHEDULE EVENTS** — `subscription_schedule.created` / `.updated` / `.released` / `.canceled` / `.completed` / `.aborted` (`SCHEDULE_EVENT_TYPES`; `HANDLED_EVENT_TYPES` exactly ten) — because a schedule can change without any field on the subscription itself changing, so `customer.subscription.updated` alone can miss it: `subscription_id_of` reads a schedule event's `subscription`, else `released_subscription`, else None — a schedule naming neither is a 200 with nothing done and no "handled" line; each handled one is the same nudge (fetch by id, upsert, the same 503 on a fetch failure, the same 200 and ERROR on `resource_missing`, one INFO line with the type and id); **not `.expiring`**, a seven-day reminder nothing reads, pinned unhandled. **No permission change**: Stripe maps the schedule endpoints to `subscription_read`, which the key holds at Write. **THE VERIFIER → 0031, 271** (ten new, not eight — below): both columns nullable (the column loop), `scheduled_plan` on `billing_plan` by `udt_name` and `scheduled_plan_at` timestamptz, no server default on either (`.first()`), the CHECK by name, the pair invariant as data (guarded on the columns), and `next_allowance` / `scheduled_allowance` absent — negative-tested by a temporary downgrade in the 0031 round trip (262 / 9 at 0030; the 0030 round trip now reads 259 / 12 at 0029, 0031's nine failing beside cancel_at's three) and by a plant with the CHECK dropped and recreated (269 / 2: the shape line and the data line). **THE RIDER RAN — prerequisite 3 was met**: `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` 1.176.0 records `(gt)` PASSED on 2026-10-19 (capture 19:55 UTC, switch 20:17 UTC, startup `sweep=on`, two rows destroyed as predicted), so `render.yaml`'s `SWEEP_ENABLED` is `"true"` with its comment rewritten — the file says what the worker does, and a Blueprint sync can no longer put the dashboard back to off; **`WorkerSettings.sweep_enabled`'s default is untouched** (a local worker and the suite stay dark; the field-list pin at seven and its `default is False` unedited; the one comment in `test_worker.py` that said the slot was declared `"false"` restated). **The moved pins, each with its reason in the test**: `GET /billing`'s body key set (gains `scheduled_change` — three exact-dict pins); the fixtures — `_plant_subscription` gains the two columns and `_subscription_object` emits `schedule` (null, an id, or the expanded object) as Stripe always sends it; the 0030 round trip's downgraded tally (257 / 4 → 259 / 12 — a downgrade to 0029 passes through 0031); `test_photo_count.py`'s `HEAD` → 0031 with its comment; the tallies in `test_co_hosts.py`, `test_destruction.py`, `test_failure_reason.py` and `test_billing.py` 261/260/259/258 → 271/270/269/268, their comments extended; every existing `caplog` test unedited and green. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the verifier adds TEN lines, not eight — two never-stored columns asserted absent in CK-73's own discipline, so every tally moved by ten; (2) the boundary for a schedule with no current phase (`not_started`) is the item's period end — the kickoff's rule names the current phase's end alone, which such a schedule lacks; (3) an unexpanded schedule or price is folded into the same ERROR line as an unknown key rather than silently null — one new log line, not two, and a broken expansion stays visible; (4) `reference/operations/deploy.md`'s *Verifying a deploy* step 5 still said the verifier reads 258 / 0 — stale since CK-73.1 — and is corrected to the head's total (touched beyond the rider); (5) the record's DATA-HANDLING clause, this file's Commands comment and its Deploys worker bullet were touched beyond the kickoff's list because each stated the old shape as current (the living-description rule). Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks (iw)–(iz) are Steven's, sandbox and test cards only; **(iz) is DATED, on or after 2026-11-12** — the renewal-time switch the record has called unverified since CK-73. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-73.2-report.md`.
- **1.71.0** (2026-10-12): Phase CK-73.1 — **the API's own log lines reach the deploy, and a portal cancellation is recognised** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md` 1.4.0 — §7 and §11 as amended; the two defects CK-73's deploy checks (iq) and (ir) found on 2026-10-11/12; **backend only: a new `app/logging_setup.py`, `main.py`, `models/billing.py`, migration 0030, `services/billing.py`, `api/billing.py`, `scripts/verify_schema.py` (258 → 261) and tests — nothing under `frontend/`, neither lock touched, the worker untouched**; **585 backend tests**, 6 new in `tests/test_billing.py`, with the moved pins listed below; two commits, one per root). **DEFECT 1 — THE API WROTE NONE OF ITS OWN INFO LINES**: check (iq) looked for the webhook's *one INFO line per handled event* in Render's log and found uvicorn's access lines alone. The web process never configured logging — `app/worker.py::main` calls `basicConfig(level=INFO, stream=stdout)` for the worker, and nothing in the API does — so `covey-keep.billing`'s INFO records reached a root logger at Python's default WARNING with no handler and were dropped; its WARNINGs surfaced only through the last-resort handler. **Every INFO line the API was ever meant to write had been silent**, CK-73's the first anyone looked for. **THE FIX, decided by the planning session**: `app/logging_setup.py::configure_logging`, called once at `app.main`'s import — the **`covey-keep` namespace** at INFO with ONE marked handler to stdout in the worker's format (`LOG_FORMAT`, pinned equal to the worker's source literal), idempotent (a second call, a reimport, uvicorn's reloader: the mark is found and nothing is added — pinned in-process and in a fresh interpreter through `importlib.reload`); **NOT the root** (only our lines become visible, never a library's INFO line by accident — the `stripe` WARNING and `botocore` pins stand, pinned); **`propagate` stays True** (the suite's `caplog` captures at the root, and CK-73's never-log pins would pass VACUOUSLY on a namespace that stopped propagating; in production the root has no handler — pinned in a fresh interpreter: WARNING and zero handlers before and after `import app.main`, the line on STDOUT alone); the worker untouched and never loading the module (pinned beside the CK-59 graph pin). **THE ENUMERATION** (STEP 1, the report): every INFO-or-above call under `app/` the web import graph reaches — `app.services.ingest` and `app.worker` are not in it — is on `covey-keep.billing`: eight at CK-73 and one new, carrying event ids, subscription ids, plan names and a Stripe status word; none can carry an email, a URL, a key, a payload, a filename, a caption or a tag. **DEFECT 2 — A PORTAL CANCELLATION WAS NOT RECOGNISED**: check (ir) cancelled in the portal, which read *Cancels Nov 12* with the subscription `active`, and `GET /billing` still said `cancel_at_period_end: false` — **under `2026-09-30.endive` the portal cancels by setting `cancel_at` to the period-end instant and leaving the boolean false**, and `sync_subscription` read only the boolean; the allowance and status were right throughout, the "this plan is ending" fact lost. (Cancelling also released the scheduled downgrade — the `schedule` went null — harmless and recorded.) **THE FIX**: **migration 0030** — `subscriptions.cancel_at` timestamptz NULL, no default, **nothing backfilled** (a migration cannot ask Stripe; the next event writes it, and on the deploy check (iv) does), invisible to the old instance under the pre-deploy ordering, the downgrade dropping it; `sync_subscription` writes it through `_field` as an instant from Stripe's epoch seconds, null written like any value ("Don't cancel"); **`GET /billing` carries `ends_at` in place of `cancel_at_period_end`** — `_ends_at`: `cancel_at` when set, else `current_period_end` when the boolean is true, else null — derived from the two facts stored verbatim and never stored itself, so the one reader (CK-74's plan screen) gets the answer rather than two fields to combine; the allowance reads neither (a plan with an end date is paid until Stripe says it is not — pinned). **THE RIDER, the record's own rule applied to the fetch**: a `resource_missing` from `fetch_subscription` (which now answers None, `close_customer`'s shape) is a finding a retry cannot fix — a **200**, nothing written, one ERROR line naming the event and subscription ids and no "handled" line, never the 503 that would have Stripe retry for days; a permission refusal and the network down are still the 503 (check (is) showed the deletion path does not reach it; the gap was the fetch). **THE VERIFIER → 0030, 261**: `cancel_at` nullable (the column loop), `timestamp with time zone` and no server default (`.first()`), negative-tested by a temporary downgrade in the 0030 round-trip test — the verifier failing on exactly four lines, the head and the three. **The moved pins**: `GET /billing`'s body key set (`cancel_at_period_end` → `ends_at`, with its reason in the test); `test_photo_count.py`'s `HEAD` → 0030 with its comment; the tallies in `test_co_hosts.py`, `test_destruction.py`, `test_failure_reason.py` and `test_billing.py` 258/257/256/255 → 261/260/259/258, their comments extended; every existing `caplog` test unedited and green. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) prerequisite 1 was NOT met — the Hub was clean but one commit ahead of its remote (the planning session's own commit, which wrote this kickoff); the push is Steven's, the CK-66/CK-68 precedent applied, the breach heads the report; (2) the record was touched beyond the two banners the kickoff named — a third, short banner before §9 (its CK-73 banner said *no column beyond §9's list*, false once 0030 adds one) and one clause in DATA-HANDLING (*the cancel flag* — now the cancellation date too), the living-description rule; (3) the enumeration counts nine lines, not eight — the rider's ERROR is the ninth and is this phase's own. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks (it)–(iv) are Steven's, sandbox and test cards only; (iv) re-syncs the one subscription so its row gains its `cancel_at`. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-73.1-report.md`.
- **1.70.0** (2026-10-11): Phase CK-73 — **paid tiers, the backend: two photo plans on Stripe, and the allowance read from a local row** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md` 1.2.0 — the backend half BUILT; the frontend is CK-74; **backend only: `requirements.in` + both locks regenerated, `config.py`, `models/enums.py`, a new `models/billing.py`, migration 0029, `services/keeping.py`, a new `services/billing.py`, a new `api/billing.py` (the eleventh router), `api/profile.py`, `main.py`, `render.yaml`, `.env.example`, `scripts/verify_schema.py` (219 → 258) and tests; NOTHING under `frontend/`** (177 unchanged); **579 backend tests**, 43 new in `tests/test_billing.py`, with the head and tally pins moved (listed below); two commits, one per root). **THE DEPENDENCY, ON THE LOCKED TREE**: `stripe==16.0.0` (uploaded 2026-10-01, ten days before this phase; API version **`2026-09-30.endive`**, the SDK's own constant, which the webhook endpoint is registered against — it equals the account default the record §2 records, and the code relies on the constant, not the account's setting) with the fourth deliberate-exception comment in `requirements.in`; both locks regenerated with the documented commands, **42 → 46 packages and NO already-locked version moved** — proved by a parse of both locks before and after: the four new are `stripe` 16.0.0, `requests` 2.34.2 (2026-05-14), `charset-normalizer` 3.5.2 (2026-09-30) and `certifi` 2026.7.22 (2026-07-22, already in the dev lock at that version — a scratch constraints file held it there, and the unconstrained recompile INTO THE SEEDED OUTPUT reproduced the lock byte-identical outside one `# via` line, the CK-72 proof; a keeper, an invitee, a co-host and a stranger saw nothing before and see nothing now; a guest upload's failed row (`is_uploader` NULL) is visible to nobody — recorded, the guest flow unbuilt; the queue never held one. **DISMISS IS A HARD DELETE, THE UPLOADER'S**: `POST /media/{id}/dismiss` → 204, no body — through `_visible_media` first (the 404 for anyone who cannot see the row), **409 `not_failed`** carrying the rung for a row that has not failed (the one new code; not `not_ready`, which means "wait"), then ONE guarded `DELETE … WHERE id AND status = 'failed' AND uploader_person_id = <the caller>`, a rowcount of 0 the 404; the tags go by the CASCADE; no store call (the router still imports neither `ingest` nor a storage delete — pinned); no count moves (`photo_count`, `total_bytes`, the keeper's usage — pinned); nothing logged; no `dismissed_at`, no bin, no window, no reap — a failed row holds nothing but the person's own filename and tags, and the operator's record is the worker's dead-letter WARNING line. **THE BACKFILL**, the one-time exception to "never derived": STEP 0 enumerated every text a `failed` row could have carried since CK-35 from `ingest.py`'s history — four texts, every prefix unmoved across the thirteen commits that touched the file (the table is in the report) — and 0028 classifies by prefix, `too_large` before the general `unreadable`, reporting the count per reason and the count LEFT NULL on alembic's INFO line (zero expected; a non-zero count the finding the invariant fails on); `last_error` untouched. **THE ENUM-LABEL TRAP, MET FROM THE OTHER SIDE (the Gotcha below)**: the first draft's `WHERE status = 'failed'` ran clean on the dev DB and FAILED THE SUITE — on a fresh database every migration runs in one transaction, and 0016's `'failed'` label is itself uncommitted there (*unsafe use of new value*), while on the dev DB and the deploy it committed on 2026-09-07; measured on PostgreSQL 16.15, a label added by `ADD VALUE` is unsafe in its transaction even when the type was created in it, which makes 0025's "a fresh test database is the one exemption" the wrong way round; the backfill compares `status::text` (the same rows, the label never constructed), and the new type's own labels are safe (made by `CREATE TYPE`). The round trip — down to 0027, six planted texts plus a `ready` row and one text the code never wrote, up, down, up — is a test, the INFO line pinned verbatim with `1 failed row(s) left NULL`. **THE FRONTEND**: `MediaItem` gains `failure_reason`; `lib/media.ts::failureMessage` and `failureRetry` turn it into the row — *This photo is too large to keep here — it's over 50 megapixels.* + *A smaller copy will work.* (the uploader alone) with **Choose a smaller copy** and **Dismiss**; *This file isn't a photo we can read.* with **Dismiss** alone; *This upload didn't finish.* (exactly the device-side line) and *We couldn't finish preparing this photo.* with **Try again** and **Dismiss**; the fallback *We couldn't process this photo.* with Try again and Dismiss — the number from `MAX_PHOTO_MEGAPIXELS` (50) beside the copy, its source `processing.MAX_PIXELS` named; **every control on a failed row is the uploader's** (`is_own`, pinned with a non-own fixture — the gate does not lean on the server's audience); - **1.67.0** (2026-09-30): Phase CK-70 — **a JPEG that carries a second image is still a photograph: decode MPO's primary frame** (found at CK-69's checks — `IMG_1721.jpeg`, row `9ad10eeb-…`, 1,807,499 bytes, declared `image/jpeg`, dead-lettered *could not process the upload as a photograph: MPO is not a supported photograph format (permanent)*; **backend only: `services/processing.py`, two comments in `api/media.py`, `tests/fixtures/media/` and tests — NO migration, head stays 0027, the verifier at 211 unchanged, NO frontend change, NO change to the intent allowlist or any refusal copy**; **515 backend tests**, 3 new — `test_processing.py` 2 (the MPO fixture through the parametrised GPS test, and the MPO test of its own), `test_worker.py` 1 — with **one pin changed deliberately**, below; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT**: some JPEGs carry more than one image — the Multi-Picture Format (MPF, CIPA DC-007), which phones write to embed an HDR gain map or a portrait depth map after the photograph — and **Pillow names such a file `MPO`, not `JPEG`**; `DECODABLE_FORMATS` was `{JPEG, PNG, WEBP, HEIF}`, so the intent's `image/jpeg` admitted it and the decoder refused it: the allowlist and the decoder disagreed, which CK-36's pin exists to prevent and could not see, because it pinned format NAMES rather than what a phone writes under an admitted type. Deterministic, not transient (a later upload that worked was a different copy; the deployed decoder unchanged since 2026-09-16). **STEP 1, reproduced and enumerated before anything changed**: a generated two-image MPO drew the exact deployed message from the unmodified `render_layers`; then every format name `Image.open` can report for bytes a phone would label with one of the five admitted types was enumerated from the INSTALLED Pillow 12.3.0 and pillow-heif 1.7.0 — 26 samples: baseline, progressive, EXIF-bearing, ICC-bearing and 4:4:4 JPEGs, two- and three-image MPOs, an Android Ultra HDR JPEG, PNG and APNG, lossy, lossless, EXIF-bearing and animated WebP, pillow-heif's HEIF, the committed HEIC fixture and the same container under every HEIF major brand — with every registered opener's `accept` listed per sample: `image/jpeg` → `JPEG` or `MPO`; `image/png` → `PNG`; `image/webp` → `WEBP`; `image/heic`/`heif` → `HEIF`; nothing else, so nothing else was admitted. **THE FIX**: `DECODABLE_FORMATS` gains `MPO` and its comment is rewritten to list the real names per admitted type (the MPF; the primary frame as the photograph; **only frame 0 ever decoded — nothing seeks**; every other image dropped by the rule that drops every other embedded byte; an HDR gain map therefore not applied, the layers the standard-range base image; the pixel guard reading frame 0's dimensions); `api/media.py`'s allowlist comment and module docstring gain the missing clause — **one content type can arrive as more than one real format name**. **THE FIXTURE, the CK-36 discipline**: `gps-mpo.jpg` — `gps-oriented.jpg`'s primary frame exactly (same pixels, same GPS IFD and orientation 6, same quality) with a small solid-GREEN second image appended through Pillow's MPF writer; green rather than the kickoff's suggested magenta, because the primary's pixels are red and blue, whose every blend and compression artefact keeps green near zero (measured: the layers' highest green channel is 28 of 255), so one green pixel in a layer is the second image showing through; regenerated by `make_fixtures.py` with the other three fixtures byte-identical. **The pins**: the fixture first shown to be what it claims (MPO, two frames, GPS and orientation 6 in the primary, the second frame green); three layers, each single-frame and the layer's own format, no MPF marker, GPS absent both ways, upright pixel by pixel, not one green pixel; **and BYTE-IDENTICAL to the JPEG fixture's layers** — which is how `draft`, `load` and `exif_transpose` treating an MPO exactly as a JPEG (`MpoImageFile` subclasses `JpegImageFile`) is shown rather than asserted; the module's code never names `.seek(` (comment lines excluded); and the worker publishes the MPO fixture end to end — `ready`, attempts 1, three derivative rows, `live` in the open fixture gathering, every stored layer single-frame, MPF-free, GPS-free and upright. **The changed pin, with its reason in the test**: `test_the_decodable_formats_are_exactly_the_allowlists_real_counterpart` → the five names, its decode loop now one sample per name, each first shown to carry that name (a generated MPO among them; the HEIF sample the committed fixture). Unchanged and passing: the GIF/BMP/TIFF refusal, the pixel guard, the truncated-JPEG and renamed-`.mov` refusals. **Recorded, not acted on**: Pillow 12.3.0 reports an Android Ultra HDR JPEG (MPF plus an `hdrgm` XMP) as plain `JPEG`, base image only — a later Pillow naming it `MPO` lands in the same set with the same outcome; and Pillow's native AVIF plugin also accepts a `mif1`/`msf1`-branded HEIF, but its factory raises `SyntaxError` on one, so `Image.open` moves on to pillow-heif whatever the registration order (in the worker's process `HEIF` is registered first regardless — `processing.py` registers it at import, before Pillow's own plugins). **Not done, by the kickoff**: no `failed` row revived — `9ad10eeb-…` stays `failed`, its original deleted at the dead-letter; the person uploads again. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) green, not magenta, above; (2) this file was touched beyond the kickoff's three items — the directory map's `tests/` and `fixtures/media/` entries describe the fixture set and the two test files and would otherwise state three fixtures (the CK-62/CK-65 precedent: living descriptions must not state the old behaviour); (3) `api/media.py`'s module docstring was amended beside the comment the kickoff named — two claims about one rule must agree. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks (ie)–(ig) are Steven's, on the same copy of `IMG_1721.jpeg` (its `upload_size_bytes` of 1,807,499 is what proves it). a co-host's seat is `isHost={false} organises={true}`). **THE REMOVED VIEW LISTS GATHERING-BIN ROWS ONLY, AND EVERY ROW IN IT IS TREATED AS ONE, WHOEVER UPLOADED IT**: an organiser's own photograph that another organiser removed renders the gathering-bin line and the gathering-bin controls there — never "In your bin", never the personal controls (`mediaStateMessage`'s `gatheringBin` flag; `binnable` excludes the view); outside it an own removed row is the personal bin, as CK-64 built it. **CO-HOST MANAGEMENT, inside the Invitations section and nowhere else** (`lib/coHosts.ts`: the types, `addCoHost` / `removeCoHost`, `coHostRefusalMessage`, the copy): the co-host list loads with the invitations when the section opens, above Accepted; the host reads the hint — *"A co-host can edit this gathering and its dates, invite people, see every answer, and take photos off the gathering. Only you review photos, delete them permanently, and choose co-hosts."* — gets **Make co-host** on each Accepted row not yet a co-host (one click, posting `{"invitation_id": <accepted.id>}` — the one handle the host already reads — then reloading the list; "— Co-host" beside a co-host's name instead), and **Remove as co-host** behind a second step (*"{name} will no longer be able to edit this gathering, invite people, or take photos off it."* — Remove as co-host / Keep as co-host); a co-host reads the list read-only with **Step down** on their `is_self` row alone, behind its step (*"You'll no longer be able to edit this gathering, invite people, or take photos off it. You can put it back for 30 days."*, co-host gated *"…If you put it back within 30 days, the host will look at it again before anyone else sees it."* — it stopped naming the uploader, because "{who} included" was false where {who} is an organiser; - **1.65.0** (2026-09-29): Phase CK-68 — **co-hosts: the relation, the two helpers, and every check classified** (`decisions/2026-09-03-co-hosts.md` §1, §4, §5, §7, as amended by two-bins §4 — the backend BUILT; the frontend is CK-69; **backend only: migration 0027, head 0027; the verifier 197 → 211**, 14 new, each on the relation's shape plus one invariant, negative-tested with a plant (210/1, exit 1) and re-run as a subprocess in the suite; frontend untouched, nothing under `frontend/` in the diff; two commits, one per root). **THE SHAPE**: `gatherings.host_account_id` keeps its shape and its meaning — the host stays singular and stays the consent controller — and a co-host is **a row in `gathering_co_hosts` beside it** (PK `(gathering_id, account_id)`, keyed on the ACCOUNT as the host column is; `added_by_account_id` as provenance; three FKs with no delete rule; an index on `account_id`; no capability column, ever — one flag and a reserved list, never a permission matrix); no expand/contract split, because nothing reads the table before this phase's own code. **THE TWO HELPERS, named for what they decide** (`api/gatherings.py`): `is_host` — the RESERVED set (a NULL host is nobody) — and `may_administer` — the host OR a co-host — with SQL forms `co_hosts_gathering` / `administers_gathering`; the loaders renamed for the set they check, `_gathering_for_organiser` / `_occurrence_for_organiser` (delegable) and `_gathering_for_host` (reserved), and **every call site moved to one or the other on purpose** (no bare "admin" names a gathering role anywhere in the backend). **THE CLASSIFICATION, verified against the code** (one line per check in `reference/backend/api-reference.md`'s Co-hosts router): DELEGABLE — the gathering's PATCH (every field but the review switch), occurrences (add, move, remove — and the RSVP-count disclosure its 409 carries), invitations (create, list, revoke — the revoke had inlined its own host comparison, not a loader), the full RSVP list in every mode, taking a published photograph down, reading the gathering's bin and restoring from it, the co-host list, stepping down; RESERVED — `pending` visibility, the queue, publish, decline, the batch, the review switch (**the PATCH is field-level**: a co-host sending `requires_approval_override` draws a 422 on that field and the whole request is refused, nothing else applied), destroying someone else's photograph (emptying the gathering's bin included), adding or removing co-hosts; UNCHANGED — the upload (the interim read audience), a person's own photographs, the read audience itself (a co-host is in it by construction, and the relation grants no read), the keeper's audience. **`api/media.py`**: `_moderates` IS the SQL `may_administer` — CK-66's one named place, widened as its docstring promised — so a co-host reads the gathering's bin (`?removed=true`, `/url`) and takes a published photograph down (the co-host as remover); **`_may_remove` split** into `_may_send_to_bin` (host, co-host, uploader) and `_may_destroy` (host, uploader — never a co-host), so `remove` widened and `destroy` did not; **a co-host's restore passes through the gate** — `restore_media`'s third path, the uploader's destination with the gathering-bin restorer condition, one guarded UPDATE per path, `_gate_destination` extracted so the two non-host paths share one reading of the ladder (gated → `pending`, back to the host and out of the co-host's own sight; open → `live` with a NULL publisher on a never-published row); the local `_is_host` deleted — one definition, imported. **THE THREE ENDPOINTS** (`api/co_hosts.py`, the tenth router): `POST /gatherings/{id}/co-hosts` — the host alone, the subject an accepted invitee named by **the accepted invitation's id, the one handle the host already reads** in `GET …/invitations` (never an email, a person id or an account id), 422s with stable codes `not_invited` / `is_host` / `already_co_host` / `account_deleted` (the last a check the planning list did not name: deletion ends co-hosting and an add must not undo it), everyone else the gathering 404; `GET /gatherings/{id}/co-hosts` — the organisers, display names and the handle, `is_self`; `DELETE /gatherings/{id}/co-hosts/{invitation_id}` — the host removes anyone (idempotent), a co-host removes exactly themselves, a co-host naming another is hidden (the co-host 404). **`caller_role`** — `host` | `co_host` | `null` — on the create, detail, list (an `EXISTS` column in the one statement; the CK-20 pin passes unedited) and patch bodies; never the co-host list itself. **The deletion leg** (`services/keeping.py::lapse_kept_statuses`, a fourth write): the account's co-host rows DELETED in the transaction that relinquishes host — a row is the role, there is no "needs a co-host" state — and the rows it added stay as provenance. **A co-host row on a hostless gathering still organises** (co-hosts §7's first item, provisionally), nobody is host there, and the record's second item stays open (the answer built is no). **Recorded, not fixed**: a keeper who holds no accepted invitation has no handle the host can read and cannot be made a co-host through this surface (every keeper on the deploy is the host); the list rule's pre-existing gap — kept-or-invited since CK-49b, so a host who is not the keeper does not see their own gathering in their list (the sponsorship shape, psql-only on the deploy). **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) **the prerequisite was NOT met** — the Hub's `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` was dirty at 1.149.0 (the planning session's own entry) when the phase began; the CK-59/60/61/66 precedent applied — the file untouched, the docs commit staging its files by path, the breach heads the report; (2) `decisions/2026-09-27-two-bins.md` was touched beyond the kickoff's docs list — its §8 carried "the exact rung a co-host's restore lands on" as an OPEN item, answered by this phase, and a living open item must not stay open (a BUILT banner keyed to CK-68, 1.4.0 → 1.5.0); (3) this file was touched beyond "bump + changelog" — the CK-54 and CK-66 Deploys bullets and the directory-map entries stated the old audience as current behaviour (the CK-62/CK-65/CK-66 precedent). Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. Checks (hw)–(hz) are Steven's. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-68-report.md`.

Neither will {who}: nobody in this gathering will see it again."* — true at CK-63, when the gathering's bin had no reader, and false since CK-66/CK-67: a declined photograph is in the gathering's bin, which the host opens in the Removed view and can Put back from for 30 days, so the sentence told the one person who holds a way back that there was none. The project's recurring defect class: copy that stays up after the case beneath it changes. **THE FIX**: the non-own branch reads *"The gathering won't see this photo, and neither will {who}. You can put it back for 30 days."* — the Remove step's second sentence exactly, the days from `REMOVED_BIN_DAYS` as on the other two lines; the shared first clause loses its full stop and each branch carries its own punctuation, so **the own-photo branch renders byte-identical** — *"The gathering won't see this photo. You can still see it yourself for 30 days."* (a host declining their own photograph is its remover and its uploader, so it is in their own bin). The block's comment is rewritten: the confirmation names the host's own way back, because since CK-66/CK-67 the host has one, and it promises the uploader nothing. The own-photo pin is byte-identical and unedited, and so is every other assertion in the test, the test's title (still true) and every other test. **Red first**: the moved pin failed against the unfixed component, reading the old sentence, before the component was touched. `reference/frontend/components.md` 1.22.0 → 1.22.1 — **its decline bullet had been stale since CK-63**, still quoting CK-43.1's *"…can still see it for 30 days; you won't see it again here."* and "the contributor's bin"; it now states the CK-67.1 sentence, the gathering's bin and the sentence's history. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) this file was touched beyond "bump + changelog" — the CK-43.1 Deploys bullet said the decline *says the contributor keeps it for 30 days*, stale since CK-63; it is annotated in place (*restated at CK-67.1*, the CK-44 idiom), its original text standing (the CK-62/CK-65/CK-66 precedent: living descriptions must not state the old behaviour); (2) the kickoff said the replacement "matches CK-67's Remove step, which states the same situation in the same words" — the SECOND sentences match exactly, while the Remove step's first reads *"Nobody in this gathering will see this photo, {who} included."*; the kickoff's exact wording was followed and the Remove step left untouched (its DO NOT). - **1.62.0** (2026-09-28): Phase CK-66 — **the gathering's bin: the host reads it, restores from it, and empties it one photograph at a time** (`decisions/2026-09-27-two-bins.md` §1 and §4's host half BUILT; **backend only: `api/media.py` and tests — NO migration, head stays 0026, the verifier at 197 unchanged; NO frontend change — the surface is CK-67**; **493 backend tests**, 14 new in `tests/test_gathering_bin.py`, **five existing expectations changed deliberately, each with its reason in the test**; two commits, one per root). **THE WIDENING IS ONE SEAT**: `_visible_media`'s `removed` branch gains branch (b) — the HOST, where the RECORDED remover is someone other than the uploader (a moderation removal or a decline), within the same `REMOVED_BIN` window — behind **`_moderates`**, ONE named SQL expression, so co-hosts (read and restore, never empty — co-hosts §4 as amended by two-bins) widen exactly one place; `destroy` never reads it (it stays behind `_may_remove`), so widening `_moderates` can never widen who destroys. - **1.59.0** (2026-09-28): Phase CK-64.1 — **the bin never promises more days than it has** (the one wording defect CK-64's deploy checks found; **frontend only: `lib/media.ts` and `lib/media-bin.test.ts` — NO backend change, no migration, head stays 0026, the verifier at 197 unchanged**; **134 frontend tests**, 2 new, **no existing test edited**; build + lint clean; two commits, one per root). **THE DEFECT**: check `(he)` passed on data, but a photograph binned seconds earlier read *“you can put it back for 31 more days”* against a 30-day window. `binDaysLeft` counts from `removed_at`, stamped by the SERVER's clock, to `now`, read from the DEVICE's, and rounds up. When the device trails the server by even a second, a fresh removal has the whole window plus that second left, and `Math.ceil` made it 31. CK-64's tests read both instants from one clock, so none could see it. **Not a timezone bug**: `removed_at` is `timestamptz`, serialised with its offset. **THE FIX IS ONE CLAMP**: the counting path returns `Math.min(REMOVED_BIN_DAYS, Math.ceil(remaining / DAY_MS))`. Rounding up stays below the cap (29.9 days left still reads 30), the `< DAY_MS → 0` and `null` paths are unchanged, and the copy, the rounding direction and every other function are untouched. The comment above it names the two clocks and the cap. It also says why a device clock AHEAD of the server's is the acceptable direction: it can read one day short near a day's turn and at the end, which under-promises. Nothing corrects for skew, and no server time is sent to do it; the server's audience rule decides when the way back closes. One residue is recorded in the comment rather than fixed: below the cap, a trailing clock delays each day's tick by the skew, as a page left open delays it anyway. Only at the top could it name a number the window never holds. **Tests** (`lib/media-bin.test.ts`, appended after the existing bin-line test, which is untouched): `now` one second, one hour and one day BEFORE `removed_at` reads 30, and 29.9 days left reads 30 (the cap takes nothing from ceil); the bin's line from a clock one second and one hour behind reads *“…for 30 more days.”* from both seats. **Both failed first against the unfixed code, reading 31**, before the clamp was written. The existing cases (30 at removal, 28 at 2.5 days, 1, 0, null) pass untouched. `reference/frontend/components.md` 1.21.0 → 1.21.1 adds one sentence to the days-left rule. This file's Deploys bullet and directory map still describe `binDaysLeft` as rounded up and never negative, which stays true; they were not edited, per the kickoff's scope. Not pushed (the CK-45 precedent). Check `(hi)` is Steven's: a fresh throwaway photograph sent to the bin in the deployed app reads *“…put it back for 30 more days.”*, is put back, and no count moves. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-64.1-report.md`.

else not ready yet), `already_removed`, `not_removed`, and **`not_pending` — the lost race on ALL THREE acts** (the api-reference's `_CHANGED_WHILE_DECIDING`, not a removal-specific code) → *changed while you were looking at it — refresh the list and try again*; none claims anything was deleted or put back. **Send to bin is one click, no confirmation** — it can be undone. **Put back lets the SERVER decide where the photograph goes** (the gate at restore time — never a parameter, never "back to what it was"); where the gathering requires approval (`requiresApproval`, the effective value already on the prop) a hint beside it says so **from the reader's seat** — *The host will look at it again before anyone else sees it.* for the uploader, and for the host reading their own binned row *It'll wait for you to publish or decline it before anyone else sees it.* (the CK-43.1 seat rule: never a person waiting on "the host", which would be themselves — the host-seat line is this phase's addition to the kickoff's one string, recorded); no hint in an open gathering. **Delete permanently always takes a second, deliberate step, inline** (the decline's shape; never `window.confirm`): the bin record §7.1's sentence **character for character** — *Are you sure? This will permanently remove the photo from CoveyKeep and cannot be recovered.* — with **Delete permanently** and **Keep it** beside it, the name flowing from `brand.ts` (`DELETE_PERMANENTLY_CONFIRMATION`) and the rendered sentence pinned as a literal; "from CoveyKeep" because a printed book is out of reach; the step stays open while its request runs ("Deleting…") and closes with the outcome; **the editor and the step never share a row** (opening one closes the other). **Optimistic-free, one act at a time on the existing `actingId` guard** — plus `actingAct`, so the review's Publish label reads "Publishing…" only during a publish (until now it read so during ANY act on the row — unreachable before a bin act could share the row; a label fix, no review copy or behaviour changed); a success re-reads and the server's body renders; **a destroyed row leaves the list** (a row in destruction is visible to nobody) and a polite `role="status"` line, *Photo deleted.*, says why, cleared by the next act or the next re-read the person asks for (a poll tick is neither); a refusal stays on the row with **Refresh the list**. **THE BIN'S LINE** (`mediaStateMessage`'s `removed` branch): *In your bin. Only you can see it, and you can put it back for N more days.* — N from `binDaysLeft(removed_at, now)`, `REMOVED_BIN_DAYS` against an injectable `now`, **rounded UP to whole days**, *for 1 more day*, *for less than a day* in the last day, never negative, and no count at all for a row with no readable stamp; **it never says the photograph will be deleted, emptied or cleared after the window** — the automatic clear ships switched off and nothing clears a bin until it is on; what is enforced is that the person stops seeing it and can no longer put it back, and that is all the copy claims (the kickoff's ban list — *deleted after / emptied / cleared / forever / safe / backed up* — pinned on the rendered bin row and both confirmations). Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent; checks (hd)–(hg) are Steven's, and **(hf) is irreversible: a second fresh throwaway upload, never a photo anyone wants.** Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-64-report.md`.
- **1.57.0** (2026-09-27): Phase CK-63 — **who removed it: removal attribution, the uploader's view narrowed to their own removals, and restore from your own bin** (`decisions/2026-09-27-two-bins.md` §1–§4; **migration 0026, head 0026** — `media.removed_by_person_id`, nullable, FK → people with no delete rule, no default, NOTHING BACKFILLED; the verifier **191 → 197**, 6 new, each negative-tested on the dev DB and as a subprocess in the suite; **479 backend tests**, 13 new in `tests/test_removal_attribution.py`, **eleven existing tests edited, each for a reason it states** — three whose EXPECTATION this phase changes (the decline test, the remove test, the host-binned test: the uploader no longer sees a host's removal), six whose plant gains the remover it needed to keep meaning what it meant, and two pinned literals that follow the head — CK-57's verifier tally (191 → 197) and CK-51a's round-trip `HEAD` (0025 → 0026); **120 frontend tests**, one string pin changed; build + lint clean; two commits, one per root). **THE FACT**: two bins, and a photograph is in exactly one — the bin of WHOEVER REMOVED IT. `remove` writes the caller's id and `decline` the host's, in the SAME guarded UPDATE as `removed_at` (pinned at the cursor: one UPDATE on media per act names the remover, and it names the stamp); `destroy` and the sweep leave it alone — a destroyed row keeps its remover as provenance. **THE NARROWING**: `_visible_media`'s `removed` branch admits the uploader only when the remover is them — a photograph the host removed or declined is visible to NOBODY (the gathering's bin exists as a state with no reader; the host's view, host restore and co-hosts are later phases); a row with a NULL remover (every removal before 0026) is visible to nobody, deliberately — a self-removal and a decline cannot be told apart after the fact, and showing a declined photograph to its uploader is the harm the record exists to prevent; a host removing their OWN upload keeps it in their own bin; and an uploader can no longer `destroy` a host-removed row — it is not in their bin, so nothing but the sweep reaches such a row until the gathering's bin has a surface (recorded as a consequence, not a bug). **RESTORE** — `POST /media/{id}/restore`: the uploader alone, on their own self-removed photograph, within `REMOVED_BIN`; everyone else — the host, a keeper, a stranger, the uploader of a host-removed row — draws the media 404 byte-identical to a missing id; **the gate decides where it goes and the restorer does not** — `publication.resolve_gathering` asked at restore time through the gatherings router's `_host_kind`, the same two facts the worker's publish step reads: gated → `pending` (the host's queue, `published_at` AND `published_by_person_id` cleared), open → `live` (an existing stamp kept; a never-published row stamped `now()` with a NULL publisher); **no count moves** (`photo_count`, `total_bytes` and the keeper's usage identical across a remove-then-restore, pinned); ONE guarded UPDATE keyed on `ready` + `removed` + removed-by-this-caller, a rowcount of 0 the lost race, refused; 409 `not_removed` (the one new code) for a row not in the bin, `not_ready` for a `failed` one, a row past the window a 404; a second removal stamps a fresh window; nothing logged at DEBUG. **The frontend's one change is a copy correction**: the host's decline confirmation no longer says the uploader can still see it — *The gathering won't see this photo.
