- **1.56.0** (2026-09-25): Phase CK-62 — **the worker says it is backing off; make that true** (check (gx)'s loose end; **code root only — `app/worker.py` and `tests/test_worker.py`; NO migration, head stays 0025**; the verifier **191 → 191, unchanged and re-run**; **466 backend tests**, 4 new, **ONE existing test edited — the idle pin gains its negative half**; frontend untouched; one commit). **THE DEFECT**: `Poll.BACKOFF` was returned on `ingest.Outcome.MISCONFIGURED` at CK-35 and referenced nowhere else in the file — `run` had no branch for it, so it fell through to `await sleep(_idle_delay())`, 5 s ± 1 s, **identical to an empty queue** — while the enum comment said *sleep, retry, shout*, the log line said *the worker is backing off*, and the module docstring said the backoff was *so a mistyped dashboard value does not hammer the store*. The docstring was right about the mechanism and wrong about the consequence: backing off like an empty queue IS hammering the store when only a human editing a dashboard can clear the condition — the two halves in tension, each fine alone, which is how it survived CK-35 → CK-61. **(gx) measured it on 2026-09-25**: a rejected credential retried every ≈5 s for 24 minutes, the release line on every pass, ≈288 doomed store calls; **no data at risk** — `attempts` 0 and `available_at` fixed throughout, exactly as CK-61's release promises. The third surface in a week describing behaviour the code did not have (CK-55's label, CK-57's `last_error`). **THE FIX — one constant, one branch**: `MISCONFIGURED_BACKOFF = 60.0` beside `IDLE_POLL`, jittered the same way — `_jittered(interval)`, ± `IDLE_JITTER` as an absolute offset, because desynchronising workers that woke together is the same job at any interval; `_idle_delay` keeps its formula byte-for-byte and `_backoff_delay` sits beside it — and `run`'s loop tail sleeps `_backoff_delay()` on `BACKOFF` and `_idle_delay()` on everything else. **60 s is a choice, and the constant's comment says why**: the condition is human-clearable only; editing `R2_WORKER_*` on Render triggers a redeploy, so the interval is NEVER on the recovery path and costs no responsiveness; a minute cuts store traffic and log volume roughly twelvefold while still proving liveness to anyone watching. **`RETRY_BACKOFF` deliberately not reused** — per-row retry timing, not loop pacing — and its first rung being a minute too is recorded as coincidence, not coupling, with a source-level pin that `worker.py` never names it. **`NOT_READY` KEEPS THE IDLE INTERVAL, deliberately, and says so in four places** (the enum comment, rule 2 of the module docstring, the loop-tail comment, and a test): the worker is designed to start before the web service has finished migrating and to pick up as soon as the schema lands, and slowing it would make a documented behaviour worse. **The three surfaces agree**: the docstring paragraph is AMENDED IN PLACE (the CK-57 idiom — the CK-35 sentence stands verbatim, a paragraph beneath records what was true, what was not, (gx)'s measurement and what makes it true now); the enum's two comments say the interval each member sleeps; the ERROR line says *the worker polls again in about 60 s (MISCONFIGURED_BACKOFF, not the idle interval): only a dashboard edit clears this, and that edit redeploys the worker*. **The log's frequency is unchanged, on purpose**: one line per pass is right for an actively broken condition; the interval already made it a line a minute; rate-limiting the line is a separate question, recorded in the comment above it. **Tests** (`test_worker.py`, the injected-`sleep` pattern): the constants — 60.0, the idle pair untouched, the two bands disjoint with room, `worker.py`'s source never naming `RETRY_BACKOFF`; **the (gx) shape through `run`** — a bodiless 401 on every HEAD, four passes, every sleep inside the backoff band and outside the idle band, the row `uploaded` / attempts 0 / `claimed_at` None / `available_at` unchanged / the reason written, and four ERROR lines — one per pass — each naming the interval and carrying no key id; **`NOT_READY` at the idle band and never the backoff band**, through a real `ProgrammingError` from the claim and then through the bug path the loop coerces to `NOT_READY` — STEP 2's pin; `--once` exiting 1 on a rejected credential with nothing slept. **Existing test edited, one**: `test_idle_polling_is_five_seconds_jittered` gains *and none of them in the backoff band*, so the bands are pinned disjoint from both sides. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) the prerequisite was MET, one version past — the Hub's `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` was committed at 1.137.0 when the phase began (the kickoff said 1.136.0 modified), so nothing had to be staged around; (2) this file was touched in three places beyond "one line in the worker's section" — the Commands block's `python -m app.worker` comment and the directory map's `worker.py` entry both describe the loop's pacing and would otherwise have stated the old 5 s; (3) `reference/backend/media-pipeline.md` §5.6 (l.186) still says *a rejected credential backs the loop off like an empty queue* — owed to the planning session as the kickoff anticipated, reported and not edited. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent; check (gy) is Steven's: ≈5 release lines over five minutes, not ≈60, the row at `uploaded` / attempts 0 / `available_at` unchanged throughout. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-62-report.md`.
- **1.55.0** (2026-09-25): Phase CK-61 — **the credential-rejection class fires on a HEAD's numeric code, and the key ids are shape-checked at boot** (the highest-priority code item filed from check (gv); **code root only; NO migration — head stays 0025**; the verifier **191 → 191, unchanged and re-run** (this phase changes classification, not schema); **462 backend tests**, 11 new, **nine existing tests edited, each for one reason — the key id they name is now 32 hex**; frontend untouched; one commit). **THE DEFECT, PROVED ON THE DEPLOY TWICE BEFORE IT WAS FIXED**: CK-35's third failure class — a rejected credential releases the row unpenalized — never fired on the ingest read path, from CK-35 to CK-60, always and not sometimes. With a genuinely rejected credential (R2 answered 401, `Unauthorized`, a member of `CREDENTIAL_REJECTED_CODES`) the row climbed the transient ladder instead — attempts 1 then 2, the 1- and 5-minute rungs, `last_error` *attempt 1 of 3: storage did not answer (read: ClientError (401))*. **THE MECHANISM, verified against the installed botocore and not taken from the kickoff**: an S3 error is named from the XML body, a HEAD response has no body, and botocore's REST-XML parser synthesises `Error.Code` from the bare status — `RestXMLParser._parse_error_from_http_status` — so against one rejected credential in one process `head_object` → `'401'`, `get_object` → `'Unauthorized'`, `head_bucket` → `'401'`, `list_objects_v2` → `'Unauthorized'` ((gv)'s measurement, reproduced here with the parser: a bodiless 401 → `{'Code': '401', 'Message': 'Unauthorized'}` with `HTTPStatusCode` 401; an XML-bodied one → the named code). The ingest ladder's FIRST store call is the HEAD, and the set held named forms only, so the branch at the read site was unreachable; `_ABSENT_CODES`, seventeen lines from the set, had carried both forms (*on a HEAD the bare status, on a GET the S3 code*) for exactly this reason since CK-35 — two sets, one helper, two assumptions. **The operational sentence**: with rungs at 1, 5 and 15 minutes a mistyped key id dead-letters every queued row in about twenty-one minutes, and neither deploy run lost data only because the credential was restored in time. **THE FIX IS ONE HELPER, NOT TWO MORE MEMBERS**: `services/ingest.py::_is_credential_rejected(exc)` beside `_error_code` — True when the named code is in the set OR the response's `ResponseMetadata.HTTPStatusCode` is 401 or 403 — called at **all three** sites (read, write, destroy; the `code` local kept at each, it still feeds the failure message; only the `in` test changed, pinned at exactly three sites). The set stays named-only (its comment now says so and why); the numeric knowledge lives in the helper alone, whose docstring carries (gv)'s measurement and the reason a third classifier must call it rather than copy a mixed set. **`'400'` STAYS OUT, and the docstring says why**: a key id of the wrong LENGTH draws `InvalidArgument` / 400 — R2 length-validates before it authenticates ((gv)'s first run) — a malformed request and not a credential fact; admitting 400 would release every genuinely bad request forever. **`_ABSENT_CODES` annotated, not churned** — membership and behaviour byte-identical; the comment names it the superseded mixed form, right and left alone (storage.py tests its own copy on the HEAD, ingest.py's on the GET after it — both forms in both places because the shape is the call's), and says a third classifier reads the status the helper's way. **The module docstring's THIRD CLASS paragraph is AMENDED IN PLACE** (the CK-57 idiom): the original promise stands verbatim, and a paragraph beneath it says what was true, what was not, the measurement, and what makes it true now. **THE OTHER HALF — the three access key ids are shape-checked at boot**: `config.py` gains `_r2_access_key_id_shape` (exactly 32 hexadecimal characters, either case, no stripping — a stray space IS a mistyped value) behind a `field_validator` on `r2_upload_access_key_id` and `r2_serve_access_key_id` (web `Settings`) and on `r2_worker_access_key_id` (`WorkerSettings`) — the `database_url` pattern already in both classes; the refusal names the field, the shape and the LENGTH and never the value (the CK-36 rider's rule, kept by the message itself as well as by `hide_input_in_errors`). The module's own rule applied: fail at boot in front of whoever deployed rather than at the first claim; the upload and serve ids too, because a malformed one there fails a person's upload rather than the queue. **The secret keys are deliberately NOT shape-checked and a test pins that** — nothing has measured their format. **A real boot proves it**: `python -m app.worker --once` with a 33-character id exits 1 with *r2_worker_access_key_id … exactly 32 hexadecimal characters; this value has 33*, the value absent from the output. **THE STOP CONDITION, reported not silenced**: (gv)'s fingerprint measured the worker's id at length 32; the upload and serve ids are UNMEASURED from this machine (dashboard values), so the deploy itself is their measurement — a web-service boot failure naming either field is that finding, and the constraint is not to be loosened for it. **Tests** — `test_worker.py`: `_botocore_error(status, *, body, operation)`, a `ClientError` built by running **botocore's own REST-XML parser** over a raw response (never by hand — a bodiless response → the bare status, an XML body → the named code), beside the hand-built `_client_error` whose comment now says it is why 451 green tests never caught this (a stub that encodes the assumption of the code under test proves nothing); the helper pinned on both shapes with the SHAPE asserted first (if botocore ever changes it, that is a finding), 401/403 both ways, 400 out in both shapes, 404/500/503 untouched, a codeless stub not a rejection; **the read step releasing on a bodiless 401, a bodiless 403 and a GET's `Unauthorized`** — `attempts` 0, `available_at` unchanged, `last_error` the credential reason with neither the status nor the code in it; **a bodiless 400 and `InvalidArgument` transient and never released** (the pin that keeps 400 out); **the write step released on a bodiless 401 — its first coverage on any shape the path can deliver**; the three ids 32-hex at boot on both classes, parametrised over the three fields — accepted either case, 31 / 33 / one non-hex / a stray space each refused naming the field and the length and never the value, the secret accepted in any shape. `test_destruction.py` +1: **the destroy step released on a bodiless 403 — its first coverage**, one delete attempted, the three layers still described. **Existing tests changed, all for the same reason**: `conftest.py`'s two web ids and `WORKER_ENV`'s worker id are 32-hex fakes now (the old spellings no longer boot), the worker's through a `WORKER_KEY_ID` constant, and the seven tests that named the literal in a never-logged pin or an equality now name the constant — `test_worker_settings_expose_no_web_credential_and_settings_no_worker_credential`, `test_worker_boots_with_only_its_six_variables` (the subprocess boot, which now passes the validator for real), `test_worker_client_is_a_third_type_that_presigns_nothing`, `test_a_missing_object_dead_letters_on_the_first_attempt`, `test_an_undecodable_upload_dead_letters_on_the_first_attempt_and_its_original_goes`, `test_a_transient_error_climbs_the_ladder_and_dead_letters_on_the_third`, `test_a_rejected_credential_releases_the_row_unpenalized_says_why_and_backs_off`; no assertion's meaning moved. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) **the prerequisite was NOT met** — the Hub's `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` was dirty at 1.134.0 (the kickoff said 1.133.0 committed); the CK-57/59/60 precedent applied — untouched, this phase's files staged by path, the breach heads the report; (2) two files outside the kickoff's scope line (services, config, tests) were edited because the change made them false — `backend/.env.example`'s two key-id placeholders (`replace-me` no longer boots; an all-zero 32-hex placeholder does, with a comment) and this file's PowerShell placeholder block (the same); (3) the new tests build the exception through botocore's parser rather than the kickoff's hand-constructed dict — stronger, and the hand-built form is asserted too in the standalone proof; (4) Docker Desktop's engine stopped mid-session (the memory quirk) — restarted, the container brought back, the verifier re-run. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent; checks (gw) and (gx) are Steven's, and (gx) is the release line firing on real R2 for the first time. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-61-report.md`.
- **1.54.0** (2026-09-24): Phase CK-57 — **what a `destroying` row's `last_error` means: a released row says why, and the destroy ladder's dead-letter is asserted** (the code question CK-56 handed forward; **code root only; NO migration — head stays 0025**; the verifier **190 → 191**, one assertion added, none removed or renumbered; **451 backend tests**, 3 new, **3 existing tests edited — the first phase in five to edit one, and each moved because it pinned the behaviour this phase changes**; frontend untouched; one commit). **THE DEFECT, independent of any assertion**: `last_error` had no defined meaning — a row's value could be absent, current, or STALE (a reason from an earlier transient failure that a later release had silently superseded) and an operator reading it could not tell which: CK-55's false-label shape again. **Where it was made**: `_release_misconfigured` settled `status` and `claimed_at` alone; the module enumerated `attempts` and `available_at` as *untouched* and stopped — `last_error` was never decided, it was left. **THE SEMANTICS, recorded before the behaviour changed** (`services/ingest.py`'s THIRD CLASS paragraph, `models/media.py`'s job-bookkeeping comment): **`last_error` is WHY THIS ROW IS NOT PROGRESSING RIGHT NOW — narrower than the column's name, and both places say so** — the retry text while a row waits on a backoff, the dead-letter text when it is dead, the credential reason when it was released, NULL when nothing is wrong (`ready`, `destroyed`, a fresh mark); every outcome overwrites or clears it; the one lag is a HELD row (the claim writes nothing, the outcome will), which is why the assertions exclude held rows; operator-only, never in an API body. **The one behaviour change**: `ERROR_CREDENTIAL_REJECTED` joins the `last_error` texts — *the store rejected the worker's credential: a fact about the worker's configuration, not about the photograph; the row is queued and untouched, and runs as soon as the credential is fixed* — and the release writes it, one keyword on the existing `_settle` call, **BOTH ladders in one change** (the function already picks the rung); `attempts` and `available_at` stay untouched — *untouched* now names exactly those two. **Clearing was the other option and does not work**: a cleared released row is `destroying` / attempts 1 / no stamp / no reason — the exact shape CK-56's rejected criterion matched on a healthy row — so it fixes nothing and leaves the operator nothing. **The assertion CK-56 declined, written**: `status = 'destroying' AND attempts > 0 AND claimed_at IS NULL AND (last_error IS NULL OR btrim(last_error) = '')` → 0, labelled *every destroying row that has spent an attempt and is not held carries a last_error*, **constant-free — no `MAX_ATTEMPTS` mirrored in — and valid ONLY because the release writes a reason**, which is the point. **CK-56's `DELIBERATELY NOT ASSERTED` block is DISCHARGED IN PLACE, not deleted** (the 1.71.0 rule): a lead paragraph says asserted at CK-57, declined at CK-56, and what changed in between; the block stands verbatim beneath an *as it stood* marker so a reader can see the criterion was once wrong and why. **Negative-tested both ways on the local dev DB, each plant confirmed by its own SELECT** (the CK-56 lesson): `destroying`/attempts 1/unheld/no reason → **190/1, exit 1** on exactly the new line; a reason present → 191/0; a fresh mark (attempts 0) → 191/0; a held row → 191/0; every plant removed → 191/0, every table 0. **Tests changed, each with its reason**: `test_worker.py`'s two rejected-credential tests and `test_destruction.py`'s one — renamed (`untouched` → `unpenalized_and_says_why`) and their `last_error is None` moved to `== ERROR_CREDENTIAL_REJECTED`, because they pinned exactly the behaviour this phase changes; no other assertion in them moved. **New**: `test_worker.py` +1 — a transient failure's stale retry text REPLACED by the credential reason on release, the count and the deferral exactly where the failure left them, then cleared at `ready` (the reason is always the current one); `test_destruction.py` +2 — the same on the destruction ladder, ending at `destroyed` with the reason cleared and both invariants re-run, and **the verifier run as a subprocess against the test DB on the four plants above**, the label read from the FAIL line. **Diverged from the kickoff, recorded**: (1) `app/worker.py` is outside the kickoff's scope line (services, models, verifier, tests) and was edited anyway — its rejected-credential log line said *the row is untouched* beside a column now written, CK-55's false-label shape; it now says *released unpenalized with the reason in `last_error`*, and no test pinned the old text; (2) the class docstring line in `models/media.py` (*dead-letter reason*) was amended beside the column comment the kickoff named — two claims about one column must agree. STEP 0: both roots clean, the Hub at 1.130.0 / 1.84.0 (the prerequisite held), head 0025, 448 collected, SQLAlchemy 2.0.54 in the venv. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent; checks (gu) and (gv) are Steven's, and (gv) — the release path proved on real data — is the first time it ever will be. Report: `D:\projects\temp\CK-57-report.md`.
- **1.53.0** (2026-09-24): Phase CK-59 — **Phase B: the sweep, shipped dark** (`decisions/2026-09-20-the-bin-counts-and-empties.md` §6's automatic half BUILT; **backend only; NO migration — head stays 0025**; the verifier at **190, unchanged and re-run**; **448 backend tests**, 11 new, ONE existing test edited on the kickoff's instruction — the field-list pin, now at seven; frontend untouched; **two code commits**: the move alone, then the sweep, so the move's diff reads as a no-behaviour-change move and `git show` on it is the bisect if (gn) ever fails). **STEP 0 FIRST — `mark_for_destruction` moves out of `services/ingest.py` into a new `services/destruction.py`**, carried verbatim (docstring and both load-bearing comment blocks), and `api/media.py` imports it from there. THE COUPLING IT CORRECTS: `api/media.py` had never imported `ingest` until CK-58, and importing it pulls `services/processing.py` — Pillow and pillow-heif — into the web process, a codec stack the API never executes. Measured on the API's `import app.main`: **21 PIL modules plus pillow-heif at HEAD, zero after the move; `app.services.processing` and `app.services.ingest` no longer in the API's import graph**; resident memory on this Windows machine 97.7 MB → 95.0 MB working set after the import (four runs each; the Linux figure will differ, and the import graph is the fact). **The boundary follows the CALLER SET**: `destruction.py` holds what the API and the worker both call — the mark, and the sweep — needs the models and sqlalchemy alone, and **must never import `ingest`, `processing`, `storage` or botocore** (a Gotcha below); `handle_destroying` stays in `ingest.py`, worker-only. **`ingest.py` re-exports the name** so the ladder reads whole from the module that runs it and CK-58's three pins resolve `ingest.mark_for_destruction` unedited — the kickoff's proof: **437 passed with `git diff --stat backend/tests/` empty**, then committed alone as `6eb2f3a`. **THE SWEEP — `services/destruction.py::sweep_expired_bin(db, now, *, limit) -> int`**: SELECT at most `limit` rows that are `ready` AND `removed` AND `removed_at IS NOT NULL` AND `removed_at <= now − REMOVED_BIN`, oldest `removed_at` first, then **`mark_for_destruction` on each row individually, committing per row, counting the Trues** — **ROW BY ROW, NEVER A BULK UPDATE**: the statement's own `status = 'ready'` guard is what makes a second marker's rowcount 0 (two worker instances are one `numInstances` line away), and it only works per row; the SELECT takes no lock, because a lock would be a second safety mechanism doing the guard's job; the rows are expunged before the loop so the per-row commits never expire them (an expired attribute on an async session is a lazy load that cannot run). **It marks; CK-54's worker step destroys** — no `delete_published_object` call, no second routine, no second decrement. **The two clocks meet at one instant**: `_visible_media` shows a removed row while `removed_at > now − REMOVED_BIN`, the sweep takes it once `removed_at <= now − REMOVED_BIN` — one number (`retention.REMOVED_BIN`), two readers, no gap and no overlap, pinned. **No memorial branch, no type branch, no account branch, no fixture exemption** (§7.4, §8 — each decided, each pinned). **THE SEVENTH WORKER VARIABLE, and the amendment is deliberate**: `WorkerSettings.sweep_enabled: bool = False` — `WorkerSettings`' docstring, `config.py`'s module docstring, `render.yaml`'s worker comment and the field-list test all said *six values, and exactly six*, and all are amended in the same commit with the reason: **the six-value rule exists so the worker can never name the upload or serve credential, and a boolean grants no powers** — the credential sentence is untouched, the pin still asserts no field names a credential or the session secret, and the six-variable subprocess boot test is unchanged because **a worker booted with exactly the six IS the dark sweep**. `render.yaml` declares `SWEEP_ENABLED` with `value: "false"` — **a quoted string, one divergence from the kickoff's `value: false`**: blueprint values are strings and a YAML boolean is a type surprise on sync; pydantic reads `"false"` and `"true"` as the boolean either way, pinned. **THE WIRING (`worker.py::run`)**: `sweep_enabled: bool = False` and `clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic` join `sleep` as injectable arguments; a pass runs only when `poll_once` returned IDLE — **never between drains** — and `SWEEP_INTERVAL` has elapsed on `clock` since the last pass (the first pass at the first idle poll after boot, so an operator who turns the switch on sees the line at once); a pass that marked anything is followed by another poll, not a sleep, so its marks are drained before the loop sleeps — which makes **`--once` with the switch on the operator's sweep from a shell**; a pass that fails (schema not there, or a bug) is logged and retried at the next interval, never a hot loop, never the loop's death; the startup line says `sweep=on|off`. **`SWEEP_INTERVAL = 15 minutes` and `SWEEP_BATCH = 20`, and why**: the clock being swept is thirty days, so any interval in minutes is invisible to a person, and fifteen makes a backlog visibly move within the hour; the batch bounds what a pass puts AHEAD of a fresh upload — `claim_next` orders by `created_at` across both ladders, so a marked row, older than any new upload, is claimed first — at ≈0.55 s per destruction (CK-56's measured window) twenty is ≈11 s, inside record §6.3's 60 s p95 with margin; 1,920 a day exceeds any plausible bin exit rate by orders of magnitude, and a ten-thousand backlog (binned at once, then a month) drains in about five days, Phase C's empty-the-bin being the path for that case. **ONE INFO line per pass that marked anything** — `bin sweep: N photograph(s) marked for destruction; their retrieval window has closed` — the count and nothing else; a pass that marked nothing logs nothing. **Tests** — `tests/test_destruction.py` +6: the boundary by an injected `now` (one second inside left, the window's own instant and one second past swept; publication state and removal stamp untouched, job columns reset, the three layers left for the worker, both invariants re-run); **what the sweep takes is exactly what the uploader has stopped seeing**; the limit taking the oldest first; a `removed` row on EVERY non-ready rung never selected and nothing written; a memorial's bin sweeping exactly as a potluck's; **two sweeps over one expired row marking it once through the real guard** (the other sweep run to completion between the outer's SELECT and its mark, from a second session; one decrement; the endpoint afterwards drawing the 404). `tests/test_worker.py` +5: the numbers, and the switch reading a dashboard string both ways; **THE FLAG-OFF PROOF** — the loop driven past four whole intervals against an expired removed row with the switch at its default, the function stubbed to fail if reached and **no statement at the cursor carrying the sweep's predicate** (the flag-on test asserts the predicate IS emitted, which is what makes the negative mean something), row and counters untouched; flag on — the first idle poll sweeps, the mark is claimed and destroyed (objects gone, derivative rows gone, counters 0) before the first sleep, fourteen idle cycles pass with no pass, the fifteenth minute passes once more marking nothing and logging nothing, and the one INFO line is the exact text with no id in it; **the queue drains before the sweep** — two uploads ahead of an expired row under `--once`: PROCESSED, PROCESSED, IDLE, sweep, PROCESSED, IDLE, exit 0, no sleep; a failing pass logged twice (a `ProgrammingError`, then a bug) with the worker still polling. **DIVERGED FROM THE KICKOFF, recorded**: (1) **the prerequisite was NOT met** — the Hub's `WORKING-ON-NOW.md` was dirty at 1.127.0 (the planning session's own entry) when the phase began; the CK-60 precedent applied: proceeded with the file untouched, the docs commit staging its files by path, and the breach heads the report; (2) **STEP 1 could not be performed from this machine** — the deployed database's connection string is a live credential this session does not hold; the report carries what is reachable (the gathering id, the filenames, the archived check outcomes, the exact query) and the psql capture is Steven's, BEFORE the switch is turned on — shipping off is what keeps that ordering honest; **and the date: `removed_at` 2026-09-14 + 30 days = 2026-10-14, so a switch turned on before then marks nothing, and the two rows go on the first pass at or after 2026-10-14**; (3) the test plan's `grep -rn 'processing' backend/app/api/` returns four hits, all comments cross-referencing `processing.DECODABLE_FORMATS` / `MAX_PIXELS` (the API's allowlist must agree with the decoder, and those comments say so) — the proof is the import graph, not the grep, and it is clean; (4) `value: "false"` above; (5) `reference/operations/deploy.md` was not on the kickoff's docs list and states *six environment variables, and exactly six* — the operator's doc for the very dashboard step 6 uses, amended at 1.9.0; (6) the first heredoc'd edit script failed to parse in this Bash environment and nothing ran — every edit was re-applied from a `.py` file run by path, and every replacement asserted an exact count. STEP 0: both roots' code clean, head 0025, 437 collected, SQLAlchemy 2.0.54 in the venv (CK-60's pin honoured). Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent; test plan 5, 5b and 6 are Steven's. - **1.50.0** (2026-09-23): Phase CK-56 — **the claim clock admits the second ladder** (a verifier fix and one Gotcha; **no migration — head stays 0025**; the verifier at **190, unchanged** — one assertion narrowed, none added, removed or renumbered; **434 backend tests**, unchanged and re-run; frontend untouched; nothing under `app/` changed — the destroy path, the worker, `_claim` and `_settle` have no diff). **THE DEFECT, CONFIRMED ON THE DEPLOY AND NOT INFERRED**: `claim_mismatch` asserted `(status = 'processing') <> (claimed_at IS NOT NULL)` — an iff written when `processing` was the only claimed rung — and CK-54 added a second ladder whose rows `_claim` stamps through the same unconditional line, because a destroy job is claimed IN PLACE (`destroying` is both the queue and the claim). So **any `destroying` row fails it while a worker holds it**. Check `(gj)` planted that state on the deploy on 2026-09-23 and read **189 passed, 1 failed** on exactly that assertion, exit 1; restoring the row returned 190/0. **The window is ~543 ms per row, which is why CK-54's and CK-55's runs all missed it, and it does not stay small**: a stalled claim holds the stamp until `RECLAIM_AFTER` and needs a live worker to clear it, so a worker dying mid-destruction fails the verifier indefinitely, and Phase B's bulk sweep turns a negligible window into a routine one — the same cost CK-55 was written to remove, so it lands before Phase B rather than after. **THE NARROWING KEEPS TWO IMPLICATIONS RATHER THAN WIDENING THE EQUALITY, because it is NOT an iff on the second rung**: `processing` still implies a stamp, but a `destroying` row legitimately has none when freshly marked (the API marks straight to the rung with the job columns reset) and when it has dead-lettered (the ladder is spent and the stamp is cleared) — criterion `(status = 'processing' AND claimed_at IS NULL) OR (claimed_at IS NOT NULL AND status NOT IN ('processing', 'destroying'))`, label *claimed_at is always set on a processing row, and set only on a processing or destroying one*, the detail string and the variable name kept. Everything the old line caught is still caught: a stamp stranded on an `uploaded`, `ready`, `failed` or `destroyed` row, and a `processing` row without one. **THE SECOND ASSERTION THIS PHASE WAS COMMISSIONED TO ADD WAS NOT ADDED, and the omission is recorded in the comment block as chosen** — the count is 190, not 191. The gap is real: a spent destruction stays at `destroying` by design (CK-54 refused `failed`, which would claim the wrong job failed and would make the row visible again), so the existing `failed`/`last_error` line never sees it, and `(gj)` confirmed a `destroying` row at `attempts = 3` with `last_error` NULL fires nothing. **Both candidate criteria were rejected, the first on evidence gathered here**: the constant-free form (`attempts > 0 AND claimed_at IS NULL`) reads as strictly stronger and is not — `_release_misconfigured` settles only `status` and `claimed_at`, so a row abandoned once (the attempt counted, and BELOW the bound no `last_error` written — the claim merely re-stamped) and then released on a rejected credential sits at `destroying`, attempts 1, no stamp, no reason, **three layers intact and claimable**, which is healthy and matches; run through the real code paths and fixtures that state made the constant-free criterion count 1 and the mirrored one 0. The kickoff's reasoning was per-call and the violation is per-reachable-state, two calls apart. Mirroring `ingest.MAX_ATTEMPTS` was rejected too, by Steven: the line has **no reachable violation today** (every path to the bound writes a reason), so it is a regression guard rather than a detector and nothing loses coverage by waiting, and a mirrored bound fails SILENTLY — lowered it goes vacuous while reading as coverage, raised it fires on that released row — where `EXPECTED_REVISION` is mirrored but asserted against the database, so its drift is loud. **The real question is a code one — whether `_release_misconfigured` should write a neutral reason, or clear a stale one, so a `destroying` row's `last_error` means one thing — and once it is settled the assertion writes itself without a bound. That is CK-57, and it is a code phase.** **Negative-tested three ways on the local dev DB at 0025** (the CK-54/CK-55 practice; the empty DB needs the full accounts → people → gatherings → media chain with `uploaded_at` set, or the CK-34 assertion fails instead and the plant proves nothing): `destroying` + a claim stamp — the `(gj)` state — → **190/0**, with the OLD criterion run by hand against that same plant returning **1** and the new one **0**, so the narrowing is what changed the answer; `destroyed` + a claim stamp → **189/1** on the narrowed line, exit 1, so it did not blanket-exempt a stamp; `processing` + no stamp → **189/1**, so the surviving implication survives; every plant removed, every table back to zero, 190/0, exit 0. STEP 0: both roots clean, head 0025, 434 tests, the Hub's 1.79.0 / 1.118.0 committed by Steven as `d04db38` before the phase began. Not pushed — the CK-45 precedent. - **1.49.0** (2026-09-23): Phase CK-55 — **the CK-41 assertion, narrowed for the destruction ladder** (`decisions/2026-09-20-the-bin-counts-and-empties.md` §7.2; a verifier fix and one Gotcha; **no migration — head stays 0025**; the verifier at **190, unchanged** — no assertion added, removed or renumbered; **434 backend tests**, unchanged and re-run; frontend untouched; nothing under `app/` changed). **THE FINDING IT CLOSES**: CK-54's dev-deploy verification passed six of seven checks and failed (gf) for real — the deployed verifier read **189/1**, the one failure CK-41's *every live media row is ready* on the three photographs CK-54's verification destroyed, each `status = destroyed` with `publication_state = live`. **That is exactly what destruction is designed to leave**: `destroy` never touches `publication_state`, because bin record §7.2 keeps the emptied row and its words for the audit trail and `remove` is the act that owns that column; the row is made unreachable through `status` instead — `_visible_media`'s one `status` term. So the assertion's rationale (*a `live` row in any other rung means something wrote around the worker — a published photograph nothing can serve*) was retired by design at CK-54, which narrowed CK-32's derivative assertion for exactly this reason and left this one standing; **both count invariants pass**, so the shape CK-54 was chosen for is intact, and what was too wide was the claim that nothing needed rewriting. **The criterion becomes `status NOT IN ('ready', 'destroying', 'destroyed')` — BOTH rungs**, because a `live` + `destroying` row is legitimate for at least a worker cycle and indefinitely if the destruction dead-letters, and a verifier that failed inside that window would be a false alarm by construction. **The label is rewritten in the same edit**: its parenthetical had become precisely false of a `live` + `destroyed` row (that row *is* in the `live` state and has no layers at all), and an assertion that passes while printing a false sentence is worse than one that fails, because a failure is loud — *every live media row is ready or in the destruction ladder (nothing is published without its layers unless it is being destroyed)*, detail *N live media row(s) in neither the ready rung nor the destruction ladder*, `live_unready` kept. **No new assertion, and why** (the comment block says it in the CK-32/CK-54 house form): the excluded set already carries a STRICTER guarantee than this line ever gave it — *no destroyed photograph still has derivative rows*, CK-54's terminal half — which is the difference from CK-54's narrowing of CK-32, where the terminal half had to be created; the `DELIBERATELY NOT ASSERTED` paragraph above the line is preserved verbatim (a different question, still true). **The destroy path is untouched**: moving `publication_state` on destruction would collide with §7.2 and with what `remove` means; `api/media.py`, `ingest.py`, `storage.py` and `worker.py` have no diff. **Negative-tested both directions on the local dev DB at 0025** (the CK-54 practice — one plant passes and the other fails): a planted `live` + `destroyed` row with no derivative rows → **190/0**, and the OLD criterion run by hand with that plant in place returns **1** (the new one 0), so the narrowing is what changed the answer; a planted `live` + `uploaded` row → **189/1** on this line with the new detail, exit 1; both plants removed, every table back to zero, 190/0, exit 0. **The Gotcha, and this file's first Gotchas section**: `PUBLISHED_PREFIX = "media/"` — the constant's name says published and its value does not; `published` is the bucket, `media/` the key prefix, a published object's key `media/<media_id>/<layer>` — a check in CK-54's verification was written against the wrong path. **Why this lands before Phase B**: until it does, every deployed verifier run reports 189/1 forever, which costs an operator the one thing the verifier is for — telling a real breakage from a known one. STEP 0: both roots clean, head 0025, 434 tests collected, the Hub's 1.76.0 / 1.115.0 already committed by Steven. - **1.48.0** (2026-09-22): Phase CK-54 — **the bin arc, Phase A: a photograph's bytes can finally be destroyed** (`decisions/2026-09-20-the-bin-counts-and-empties.md` §6/§7.1/§7.2, all three BUILT here; **backend only; migration 0025, head 0025**, `alembic check` clean; the verifier at **190** (187 + 3, each negative-tested); **434 backend tests**, 25 new; frontend untouched — no route calls either endpoint). **THE FACT THIS PHASE RETIRES: nothing in CoveyKeep had ever deleted a published photograph's bytes.** `storage.py` carried one delete function and it was for quarantine originals; "remove" set `publication_state = 'removed'`, stamped `removed_at`, left `status = 'ready'`, and the three layers stayed in R2 forever — which is why neither `total_bytes` nor `photo_count` had ever been decremented, and why an allowance was a limit on what an account had *ever* kept. **Phase A of three: A (this) → B (the 30-day sweep) → C (the bin surface, which any user-facing quota surface is still gated on).** **Migration 0025, additive**: `media_status` grows to seven — **`destroying`** (claimable, retryable; `processing`'s mirror, except that `claimed_at` NULL means "marked, never claimed" because this rung is both the queue and the claim) and **`destroyed`** (terminal; `ready`'s mirror) — appended after `failed` with `ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS` and **used by nothing in the same transaction** (the CK-32 trap, which aborts a deploy and passes in the suite, because a fresh test DB creates the type in the same transaction); the downgrade drops neither label and says why (there is no honest way to reverse a destruction). **`storage.py::delete_published_object`** — the first function in this product that destroys a photograph's stored bytes: `put_published_object`'s guard (**the `WorkerClient` alone**, the one credential scoped to write that bucket — the UploadClient cannot reach it and the ServeClient is read-only, which CK-33's verifier proves against live R2) and `delete_quarantine_object`'s semantics (**an absent object is a SUCCESS**, which here is not a nicety but what makes the retry idempotent). **THE API MARKS AND THE WORKER DESTROYS, and the decrement rides the marking statement** — `POST /media/{id}/destroy` moves a `ready` row to `destroying` and drops `photo_count` and `total_bytes` in **ONE statement** (the mirror of CK-51a's increment; the byte figure summed from the row's own derivative rows inside it; **not clamped at zero** — the invariant is exact, so a negative is a finding and a floor would hide only the case where it came out right), and the job columns are reset so a photograph that took two ingest attempts still gets three destruction attempts. **NO VERIFIER INVARIANT CHANGED, and that is the argument the shape was chosen for**: the row leaves `ready` in the transaction that decremented, so `photo_count` = the count of `ready` rows and `total_bytes` = the sum over their layers both stay true — a destruction that needed a new invariant would be a destruction with a new way to be wrong. **THE ORDER INVERTS CK-36's, DELIBERATELY** (`ingest.py::handle_destroying`, stated in the code with its reason because the next reader will otherwise "fix" it): publish commits the DB *before* deleting the quarantine original, because the DB is the record of what exists and a lingering original is cheaper than a lost photograph; **destruction deletes the three published objects FIRST and commits `destroyed` after**, because commit-first plus a failed delete leaves a row claiming destruction while its bytes sit in the bucket — *after the product told someone it was gone*. Delete-first fails safe: a crash leaves `destroying`, the retry finds the objects absent, and absent-is-success makes it idempotent for free (pinned from a second session at the instant of each delete — the mirror of CK-36's ordering pin). **The derivative rows are read here and deleted in the terminal transaction, never at the mark**: their `storage_key` is what was actually written, and keeping them through `destroying` is what makes a stuck row legible to an operator. **TWO ENDPOINTS, NOT ONE FLAG** (confirmed by Steven 2026-09-22): `POST /media/{id}/remove` (to bin — **destroys nothing and frees nothing**: the layers stay, the row stays `ready`, the photograph goes on counting, bin record §3) and `POST /media/{id}/destroy` (immediate, irreversible, and it reaches a `removed` row too — the per-photograph *empty the bin*). A `permanent: true` flag means one client bug permanently destroys what should have gone to the bin, and there is no restore. **Both act on a `live` row, so this IS the takedown of a published photograph** — `media-pipeline.md` §11's item, **BUILT** — arriving here rather than in a phase of its own; **`decline` is unchanged** (pending-only, a `live` row still 409 `not_pending`): decline is the REVIEW's bottom, and two acts reaching `removed` from different places is the honest shape. **Authorization: the gathering's HOST, and the UPLOADER for their own — NOT THE KEEPER** (bin record §7.1; the removal rule — a keeper holds a reference, never a right to publish or destroy); a keeper, an accepted invitee and a stranger each draw the media 404 byte-identical to a missing id, **pinned with the keeper first shown to be inside the read audience** so the 404 is the rule and not a broken fixture. **`_visible_media` gains its one and only `status` term**: **a row in destruction is visible to NOBODY.** Bin record §7.2 keeps a destroyed row's filename, caption and tags for the audit trail (*a photograph captioned "Jenny at bat" was deleted* records what was lost where *something was deleted* does not) on the stated ground that the words are unreachable, and says that "follows from the row leaving `ready`" — **it does not follow on its own**, because every other branch of that criterion turns on `publication_state`, so a destroyed `live` row would have gone on being listed, searched and patched; writing it into the one criterion every read, the words patch and the review acts apply is what makes the requirement structural, and it also makes a destroy immediate from the caller's side (a second destroy draws the 404, not a refusal). **A spent destruction STAYS at `destroying`** with its `last_error` — never `failed`, which is the ingest ladder's terminal and would both claim the wrong job failed and **make the row visible again to the person who destroyed it** — kept out of the claim predicate by `attempts < MAX_ATTEMPTS`; uncounted, seen by nobody, owing a bucket operation an operator can retry. **A bug the tests caught**: `_release_misconfigured` hard-coded `uploaded`, right for an ingest claim and catastrophic for a destroy claim — it would have handed an already-uncounted photograph back to the INGEST ladder, which HEADs a quarantine original deleted at `ready`, dead-lettered it to `failed`, and made it visible again; it now releases to the rung the claim came from. **The verifier → 0025/190**: the two labels (negative-tested by `ALTER TYPE … RENAME VALUE`, the CK-36 precedent) and **"no destroyed photograph still has derivative rows"** — the destruction's whole point as a property of the data; **CK-32's "no derivative row hangs off a photograph that is not `ready`" is NARROWED to allow `destroying`**, with its reason in the source (the one rung at which a derivative row legitimately outlives `ready`, because the objects go before the commit), proven both ways — a `destroying` row with a layer passes, a `destroyed` one FAILs. **Tests**: `tests/test_destruction.py` (new, 24) — the one-statement decrement with the verifier's two invariants re-run as SQL after every mark, the keeper's refusal in both acts with nothing moved, the immediate invisibility from every seat and on every act, the surviving words with every search that cannot reach them, `remove` freeing nothing, `destroy` reaching the bin, `decline` unchanged, the delete-before-commit ordering observed from a second session, the idempotent retry over absent objects, the transient ladder ending at `destroying`, the rejected credential releasing to the right rung, the reclaim, the lost claim, and the log carrying no key and no word; one in `test_media_intents.py` (the singular bin form). **Three prior tests changed by the enum growing, each for the shape**: the ladder pin gains the two labels, the every-rung list test is narrowed to the ingest ladder (the destruction rungs are the deliberate exception to "every rung is listed"), `test_photo_count`'s head assertion is parameterised. **Riders** (bin record §6.1, plus four sites the fifth doc audit found that the record did not name): *"the 30-day bin"* named the wrong clock in **seven of the eight** places it appears and now says *the bin* (removed and still stored, whatever its age), reserving 30 days for the contributor's RETRIEVAL window — `tests/test_review.py`'s occurrence genuinely IS that window and is left; **`account_bin_count`'s query is byte-identical to HEAD** and must never grow a `removed_at` term; and the keeper's bin refusal had no singular branch, so one binned photograph read *"1 of them are in the bin"* — both forms now pinned. **Not built**: the sweep (Phase B — nothing here stamps a row into `destroying` on a clock, and it builds the CLOCK and nothing else; `account_usage()` sums `total_bytes` plus the in-flight reservation over the gatherings that RESOLVE to the account — ONE statement fetches the candidates with their two facts, and `resolve_keeper` decides row by row (the SQL WHERE is a superset filter, never the answer); the reservation stays inside it, the one quota path, and **the upload GATE still checks the host** (v2 §6 is the next numbered phase); `lapse_kept_statuses()` NULLs `keeper_account_id` wherever the deleted account held it — on gatherings AND on `groups.keeper_account_id` (an anonymized account cannot answer for a home's bytes; **no stamp on a group** — where that lives is v2 §12 item 9's group half, Arc B's, because no gathering resolves through a group until it writes `owning_group_id`) — relinquishes host everywhere, stamps each gathering that lost its keeper; the call site in `profile.py` untouched. **Two wiring helpers beside the resolver**: `resolved_keeper_of(db, gathering)` — a loaded row's two facts to `resolve_keeper` (`_gathering_for_read`) — and **`keeps_gathering(account_id, gathering_id)` — the ladder as ONE SQL criterion, `coalesce(the group's keeper, the gathering's own) = account`, for the WHERE clauses that decide which rows are fetched at all** (the list's one statement — the CK-20 statement-count pin holds — and `_visible_media`), safe only because the CHECK makes a both-set row impossible, pinned to agree with the Python resolver on every legal shape, **aliased so an outer `Gathering` never auto-correlates** (the bug it would otherwise be). **`grace_state` and `ARCHIVE_AFTER` / `DELETE_AFTER` UNTOUCHED** — annotated: inert (nothing but its own tests calls it), and v2 §5's five-rung ladder is behaviour, not representation, arriving with its surfaces (the in-app alert, the claim button, the storage screen; nothing past Frozen before export). `resolve_keeper` untouched in body and signature; its "not called by any consumer yet" paragraph corrected. **The three query sites** — `_gathering_for_read`'s kept check, the list's `kept_by_caller`, `_visible_media`'s `keeps` — ask the resolver; **the audience rule is byte-for-byte the same**: kept-or-person-invited-or-host, the three media branches (`live` → the read audience, `pending` → uploader and host, `removed` → the uploader for 30 days), 404-not-403 with one body for missing and non-permitted. Models: comments only (the CHECK's, both `keeper_account_id`s, `owning_group_id` — read by the resolver now, still no writer — `last_keeper_left_at`, `KeptGathering`'s docstring); `KeptGathering` and the table untouched. **No index on either `keeper_account_id`, and the reason**: an index arrives with its reader, the reader arrived in a phase that adds no migration — **owed to CK-49c's 0023** (ten gatherings do not need one; the rule does). **Tests**: `tests/test_keeper_shape.py` +3 — **THE test the phase's correctness depends on**: a gathering whose `keeper_account_id` is set and whose `kept_gatherings` row is ABSENT — the state every gathering created after this deploy is in, and nothing before now exercised — is fully visible to its keeper, first in the deployed shape (creator = host = keeper: detail, list, media list, an intent) and then with the host NULLed so the keeper rung ALONE admits (detail, list, a live photograph), the outsider's 404 byte-identical to a missing id before and after; the SQL ladder agreeing with the resolver on the three legal shapes and correlating correctly with a `Gathering` in the outer FROM; a constructed group gathering (own column NULL, host NULL, the group's keeper a second account) seen, listed and counted by the group's keeper — the first rung live for the first time — and 404 to its creator, Unkept and counted for nobody when the group's keeper goes NULL; CK-49a's two no-write pins **inverted** (creation and `keep()` write the column and NO kept row — the phase's signature). `test_keeping.py` rewritten for one keeper (a second keep refused and nothing moved; unkeep by a non-keeper loud with the keeper untouched; a kept gathering never stamped and resolving `OWN`, unkept resolving `UNKEPT`; **the sponsorship shape — host ≠ keeper — replaces every "two keepers" fixture**; the 30/90 grace assertions at lines ~106–138 byte-identical; the `kept_at` default probe kept with a note that it dies with the table). `test_account_deletion.py` (the lapse against the column — the gathering the person kept NULLed and stamped, the one they merely hosted keeps its keeper, unstamped, host relinquished on both; the group test gains the keeper: the person's own group's keeper NULLed, a group someone else keeps untouched). `test_gatherings.py` and `test_media_intents.py` read the column where they read the relation. **Two fixtures that added a second keeper to an API-created gathering — `test_media_reads._cast` and the CK-44 switch test — now MOVE the column** (host ≠ keeper): the kickoff expected fixtures calling `keep()` to need no change, and those two were exactly the many-keepers fixtures one keeper cannot represent. `test_worker.py` unchanged (its gatherings are constructed keeperless, so its `keep()` calls stand). **Verifier hazard, recorded not fixed**: `verify_schema.py`'s "no kept gathering carries a `last_keeper_left_at` stamp" assertion joins `kept_gatherings` — a gathering created between 0022 and this deploy holds a stale kept row, so an unkeep or an account deletion touching it after this deploy fails that assertion legitimately; the assertion stays (the kickoff: unchanged at 181) and CK-49c removes it with the table.
