## The directory map, as it stood in `CLAUDE.md` v1.88.0

The directory map that stood in `CLAUDE.md` until 1.89.0 (2026-10-28), verbatim, fence and every line, when `CLAUDE.md` replaced it with a map of one line per git-tracked file or folder saying what the file is now.

```
covey-keep/
├── frontend/          # Vite + React + TypeScript PWA (vite-plugin-pwa, react-router-dom)
│   ├── src/           # main.tsx (router + AuthProvider), App.tsx (route table — the catch-all at `*` rendering PageNotAvailable since CK-75.1, never a redirect; AppUpdateProvider mounted above the routes; since CK-78 `/groups`, `/groups/new` and `/groups/:id` behind RequireAuth, and until CK-79 `/groups/join` a static row rendering PageNotAvailable so `/groups/:id` never captured the join URL CK-75 mints; since CK-79 `/groups/join` → GroupJoin and `/groups/invitations/accept` → GroupInvitationAccept, both outside RequireAuth and before `/groups/:id`; since CK-80 `/groups/:id/gatherings/new` → GroupGatheringNew behind RequireAuth), brand.ts (PRODUCT_NAME/TAGLINE — the only frontend home for user-visible brand strings; CK-14), brand.test.tsx (vitest brand pins); tests sit beside their subjects — lib/*.test.ts and routes/*.test.tsx, media.test.ts + routes/media.test.tsx since CK-38 (the four states, the HEIC fallback, the video refusal, polling that stops, and no presigned URL anywhere but its one fetch — asserted against the DOM and every other call); media-words.test.ts + routes/media-words.test.tsx since CK-40 (the intent's trimmed filename and File.size, the name precedence, the caption's null-never-"", the whole tag list on every save — a second tag beside the first — and the search's debounced q with its way back); media-review.test.ts + routes/media-review.test.tsx since CK-43.1 (THE STRING both ways as literals — the waiting line where gated from every seat, CK-38's line where open; the queue absent for an open gathering host or not and for a gated non-host; the queue as the same list with the flag holding only ready+pending rows, a live and a removed row excluded, no polling; publish and decline; a 409 rendered from its code and never the server's words; the batch's item-level 422 on the row it names, nothing published, then landing whole; the stamp rendered nowhere; since CK-44 THE STRAND pinned as fixed — review off with rows waiting keeps the queue and both acts and the waiting line, one predicate at the render, all of it gone when the last row is decided — the non-host uploader's line where review is off, and the switch's two directions through the prop; gatherings.test.tsx since CK-44 carries the switch — present for the host and absent for a member, on sends exactly `true` with no statement, off shows §5's statement verbatim as a plain hint and sends an explicit null, never false); media-bin.test.ts + routes/media-bin.test.tsx since CK-64 (the three acts posting to their own paths and answering the code, one refusal line per code with not_pending the lost race, the days-left count rounded up and never negative, the bin's line in every form with nothing about the window's end, the record's confirmation verbatim; at the render — the acts on own ready rows only and on nobody else's for the host too, Send to bin one click that posts once with the in-flight guard, Delete permanently posting nothing on the first click and once on the second with Keep it posting nothing, the editor and the step never sharing a row, the destroyed row gone with "Photo deleted.", Put back's hint by seat where gated and absent where open, a refusal on its row with Refresh); media-gathering-bin.test.tsx since CK-67 (the host's side — Remove from gathering only for the host on others' live ready rows, with a non-host offered nothing and no Removed view control; the remove step's exact sentence with nothing posted on the first click, Leave it inert, and its exclusion of the editor, the delete step and the decline step; Remove it posting once with the row moving to the Removed view; the Removed view requesting removed=true and never both flags, with the personal-bin and NULL-remover rows kept out of it; the host bin line with the days left; the empty state; Put back posting restore with the hint and the publication stamp kept; the shared delete confirmation posting destroy with "Photo deleted."; and a refusal on its row from its code with Refresh; since CK-69 its stub server takes a role and a gate, and the co-host's side is pinned beside the host's — the sentences in both gate states, the Removed view's co-host line and hint by gate state, Delete permanently on their own row and never on another's, no review anywhere, the Removed view's own row for both seats, and the refusal naming the bin the act was on); co-hosts.test.tsx since CK-69 (the role matrix on the detail page — host, co-host and null, with the host's account id disagreeing with the signed-in account in the co-host case and agreeing in the null case, so caller_role is proven to decide; the co-host's edit form with no switch and a save body that never carries the override after every other field changes; management from the host's seat — Make co-host posting the invitation id once and reloading, "Co-host" beside the name, the four 422 codes and the 404 each on the row they name, Remove as co-host's two clicks with Keep as co-host inert; management from a co-host's seat — no Make or Remove anywhere, Step down on the is_self row only behind its step with Stay a co-host inert, the DELETE with their own id, the gathering reloaded and every organiser control gone; RSVP as a co-host — the full list and the total under HOST_ONLY, the label naming the organisers); lib/coHosts.test.ts and lib/gatherings.test.ts since CK-69 (the calls answering the code and never the wording, the refusal lines, the hint and the two sentences as literals; roleFlags gating closed on anything but the two known roles); media-failure.test.ts + routes/media-failure.test.tsx since CK-71 (each reason's line from both seats as literals and the fallback, the number from MAX_PHOTO_MEGAPIXELS, the controls per reason, dismissMedia posting once with no body and answering the code never the wording, the refusal lines, the ban on every line; ## Deploys bullets restated at 1.89.0, as they stood in `CLAUDE.md` v1.88.0
```

The nine Deploys bullets that carried a *Restated at CK-NN* chain or another inline amendment, verbatim and in their original order, as they stood before `CLAUDE.md` 1.89.0 (2026-10-28) rewrote each to say the current state with its corrections folded in; the other 31 bullets stayed where they were.

- Reading back (CK-37): `GET /gatherings/{id}/media` lists the photographs
  **the caller may see** and `GET /media/{id}/url?layer=web|thumbnail`
  mints one presigned GET through the serve credential for a `ready` row.
  **Who may see a photograph is decided per `publication_state`, in one
  SQL criterion both endpoints apply**: `live` → the read audience *(since
  CK-77: the host, an accepted invitee, or a member the gathering is SHARED
  WITH — never the keeper by keeping, who was a term here from CK-49b to
  CK-77; the CK-77 bullet below)*;
  **`pending` → the uploader and the host, and nobody else** *(a `failed`
  row is `pending` and the uploader's ALONE since CK-71 — the CK-71
  bullet below)* — every row
  on the dev deploy until CK-41, when the rows in gatherings that resolve
  open went `live` and the rule did not change; `removed` → the uploader
  alone for 30 days. Not interim; never widen `pending` "for now." 404
  before any other refusal; `archival` is never served (422); a row that
  is not `ready` is listed with its state and draws 409 `not_ready` for a
  URL. No endpoint moves `publication_state` (the worker does, at
  `ready`, since CK-41). A presigned GET is a bearer
  credential like the PUT — never logged, pinned with the root at DEBUG.
- The upload surface (CK-38): the **Photos** section of `/gatherings/:id`
  (`components/GatheringMedia.tsx`, `lib/media.ts`) — the first time the
  arc is usable without a REST client. Intent → PUT → confirm per file;
  **a HEIC's `file.type` is empty in the browser**, so the declared type
  falls back to the extension (it is signed into the PUT — a wrong one
  reads as an R2 permissions failure); **Content-Length is never set** by
  hand; the picker's `accept` mirrors the allowlist and is never the
  authority — a video draws the server's 422 inline, the batch refused
  whole. **A presigned URL never leaves the network call**: not into state,
  a log, an error, or the DOM — the intent response is a local of the
  handler, and a layer is fetched into a `blob:` object URL rather than
  handed to an `<img>` (which means **the published bucket's CORS must
  allow GET from the frontend origin** — a thumbnail that will not show
  while the URL endpoint answers 200 is that rule, out-of-band, not code).
  **The copy is derived from `publication_state`, never fixed**: until
  CK-41 every row was `pending` ("Only you and the host can see this");
  since CK-41 a private person-owned gathering publishes on processing
  completion (`decisions/2026-09-09-consent-gate-defaults.md` 2.0.0 §1),
  and the `live` line ("Everyone in this gathering can see it"), written
  before anything could reach it, renders with no copy change — and
  **no copy may imply a reviewer, an approval, or that a pending
  photograph is shared** — pinned at the source. `failed` offers exactly one
  affordance: try again, from the device *(restated at CK-71: the controls
  follow the row's `failure_reason` and are the uploader's — the CK-71
  bullet below)*. The list polls every 3 s only
  while something is in flight. `archival` is never requested.
- The host's review (CK-43; migration 0020): **what a host does with a
  photograph nobody has published**, decided in
  `decisions/2026-09-13-the-hosts-review.md` before it was built. **The
  host, and nobody else** — not a keeper, not a co-host, not the uploader
  on their own photograph; everyone else draws the media 404 on the queue
  and on both acts. **The queue** is `GET /gatherings/{id}/media?awaiting_review=true`
  — the `ready`+`pending` rows, a filter on `_visible_media` in the same
  `WHERE` as `q`, never a second criterion beside it; a `removed` row is
  in nobody's queue. **Publish** (`POST /media/{id}/publish`; the batch
  `POST /gatherings/{id}/media/publish`, up to 50, refused whole) moves
  `ready`+`pending` → `live` and stamps **`published_at`** and
  **`published_by_person_id`**; **decline** (`POST /media/{id}/decline`)
  moves it → `removed` with `removed_at` — the state that already exists,
  the uploader's 30-day bin intact, no fourth state; a `live` row is
  refused (the takedown is its own phase). Guarded updates with stable
  codes (`not_ready` carrying the rung, `already_live`, `not_pending`); a
  `failed` row has nothing to publish; **a `removed` row is never
  resurrected by approval**. **The publisher is NULL when the rule
  published it** — the worker stamps only the date, in the same `CASE` as
  `live`; **nothing is backfilled** (every `live` row at 0020 reads NULL
  forever), and neither column may be made NOT NULL. **Approval moves no
  byte.** The router still logs nothing. **Nothing on the deploy can be
  gated until CK-44** — the only gated subject is planted in psql on a new
  gathering and restored (checks (ed)–(eg)). *(Since CK-44 a host gates a
  gathering through the product, and nothing is planted.)*
- The review surface (CK-43.1; frontend only): **the host's review reached
  through the product** — inside the Photos section of `/gatherings/:id`,
  never a route (`components/GatheringMedia.tsx`; the helpers and every
  string in `lib/media.ts`). **One gate on all of it: the caller is the
  host AND the gathering resolves gated** — the detail body's effective
  `requires_approval` (CK-41), passed as the `requiresApproval` prop, read
  and never written *(restated at CK-44: the host AND — review on OR
  anything the host can see waiting — the switch bullet below; the
  strand rule)*. Where that is false nothing renders — not disabled,
  not hidden-but-present — and no string on the page names a reviewer, an
  approval, a queue, or what a photograph waits on (pinned at the render).
  Where it is true: **the queue is a view switch on the same list**
  ("Awaiting your review" → `?awaiting_review=true`, composing with `q`,
  the same rows through the same rendering; its empty state "Nothing is
  waiting for your review." — never "No photos yet."; **no polling** —
  only the host's act moves a queued row); **Publish** and **Decline** on
  every `ready`+`pending` row the host sees, switching on the 409's
  **code** and never its wording, the refusal kept on the row (a refusal
  re-reads nothing; a refresh control is the way on); decline on a
  **deliberate second click** that says the contributor keeps it for 30
  days and claims nothing destroyed *(restated at CK-67.1: since CK-63 a
  decline lands in the gathering's bin, never the contributor's, and
  since CK-67.1 the confirmation names the host's own way back — "The
  gathering won't see this photo, and neither will {who}. You can put it
  back for 30 days." — the CK-63, CK-66 and CK-67 bullets below; restated again at
  CK-69: the host form of the Remove step exactly, "Only you and any
  co-hosts will be able to see this photo. You can put it back for 30
  days.")*; **bulk
  approve** through the batch
  endpoint with checkboxes in the queue view, **the item-level 422 landing
  on the row it names** and "Nothing was published" beside the button.
  **THE STRING** (record §8, decided here): a gated `ready`+`pending` row
  reads CK-38's sentence and then **"Waiting for the host to publish or
  decline it."** for the uploader, **"Waiting for you to publish or
  decline it."** for the host — who is usually the uploader too. The
  publication stamp rides the body and is rendered nowhere. **No override**
  (CK-44 — built, the bullet below), no takedown, no bin browser, no
  notification copy.
- Removal and destruction (CK-54; migration 0025): **the first path in
  this product that destroys a photograph's stored bytes**, and the fact
  it retires is worth stating — until 2026-09-22 nothing here had ever
  deleted one: "remove" set `publication_state = 'removed'`, left
  `status = 'ready'`, and the three layers stayed in R2, which is why
  neither `total_bytes` nor `photo_count` had ever been decremented.
  `decisions/2026-09-20-the-bin-counts-and-empties.md` §6, §7.1, §7.2.
  **Phase A of three: A (this) → B (the 30-day sweep) → C (the bin
  surface, which any user-facing quota surface is still gated on).**
  **Two endpoints, never one flag**: `POST /media/{id}/remove` (to bin —
  destroys nothing and **frees nothing**: the photograph is still stored,
  so it still counts, bin record §3 — and **this is the takedown of a
  `live` photograph**, which nothing offered until now) and
  `POST /media/{id}/destroy` (immediate, irreversible, and it reaches a
  `removed` row too — the per-photograph *empty the bin*). A `permanent`
  flag would mean one client bug permanently destroys what should have
  gone to the bin, and there is no restore. **`decline` is unchanged** —
  pending-only, a `live` row still 409 `not_pending`. **The host, or the
  uploader for their own — NEVER THE KEEPER**, who holds a reference and
  not a right to destroy; everyone else draws the media 404 *(restated at
  CK-68: `remove` is the host's, a co-host's, or the uploader's own —
  `_may_send_to_bin` — and `destroy` stays the host's or the uploader's
  own, never a co-host's — `_may_destroy`; the keeper still neither)*. **The API
  marks and the worker destroys**: the mark moves `ready` → `destroying`
  and drops `photo_count` and `total_bytes` in **ONE statement**, in the
  transaction that moves the row off `ready` (the statement itself is
  `services/destruction.py::mark_for_destruction` since CK-58 — in
  `ingest.py` until CK-59 moved it: one copy, two
  callers — this endpoint, and the 30-day sweep since CK-59 — its own
  `ready` guard the concurrency guard, and it never commits) — so the
  room is free the
  instant the request returns and **no verifier invariant changed**,
  which is the argument the shape was chosen for *(the two COUNT
  invariants; CK-41's live-row assertion, which is not one, failed for
  real on the three destroyed rows and is narrowed at CK-55 — the
  scripts entry in the directory map)*. Then the worker
  deletes the three published objects and commits `destroyed`, **in that
  order — the INVERSE of the publish transaction's**, because a row
  claiming destruction over bytes still in the bucket is the one promise
  this routine exists to keep; delete-first fails safe, since deleting
  an absent object is a success. **A row in destruction is visible to
  NOBODY** (`_visible_media`'s one `status` term — what makes the bin
  record §7.2's surviving filename, caption and tags unreachable by any
  list, search or surface), so a second destroy draws the 404, not a
  refusal. **A spent destruction stays at `destroying`** with its
  `last_error`: uncounted, invisible, and owed a bucket operation —
  never `failed`, which would make it visible again. Not built: the
  sweep, the bin surface, restore-from-bin, a per-account bin ceiling,
  any frontend. **Recorded at CK-54, discharged at CK-66**: a host who
  chose *Send to bin* could not then destroy that row while the
  gathering's bin had no reader; since CK-66 the host reaches it again
  (the CK-66 bullet below), and the uploader never does.
- The gathering's bin (CK-66; backend only, no migration): **the host
  reads it, restores from it, and empties it, one photograph at a time**
  (`decisions/2026-09-27-two-bins.md` §1/§4's host half; the surface
  BUILT at CK-67 — the bullet below). `_visible_media`'s `removed` branch gains one seat: **the HOST
  sees a removed row whose RECORDED remover is someone other than the
  uploader** — a moderation removal or a decline — within the same
  window; the role test is **`_moderates`**, ONE named expression *(and at CK-68 it became the SQL `may_administer` — a co-host reads the bin and restores from it, through the gate)*, so
  co-hosts (read and restore, never empty — co-hosts §4 as amended)
  widen exactly one place, and never `destroy`, which stays behind
  `_may_remove`. The bin's shape is **`_in_gathering_bin`** —
  `IS DISTINCT FROM`, never `!=`, because a guest upload's NULL uploader
  would make `!=` NULL and silently hide the row. **`?removed=true` on
  the media list is the bin as a view**: gathering-bin rows only,
  composing with `q`; NO host test is written on the flag — the
  criterion composed with visibility IS the host test — so a non-host
  reads a 200 and an empty list, never a refusal that confirms the bin
  exists (deliberately not the queue's 404); **the DEFAULT list excludes
  gathering-bin rows** while personal-bin rows stay in the uploader's
  default list; the two flags together are a 422. **The host's restore
  goes `live`, whatever the gate says** — review is the host's, so
  putting it back IS approving it (two-bins §4): the publication pair
  kept WHOLE where `published_at` is set (a rule-published row keeps its
  NULL publisher — a fact, not a blank), a declined never-published row
  stamped at restore time with the HOST's person id; the uploader's
  restore is byte-identical to CK-63's. `destroy` unchanged in code —
  the host reaches gathering-bin rows because the audience shows them:
  the per-photograph empty; no bulk empty exists. **The uploader of a
  host-removed row still sees nothing, ever**; NULL-remover rows stay
  hidden from everyone; the keeper, invitees and strangers gain nothing;
  no count moves; the sweep is untouched. Detail:
  `reference/backend/api-reference.md` (the `removed` view; the restore
  section's host path), `reference/backend/media-pipeline.md` §5.16.
- The host's side (CK-67; frontend only): **Remove from gathering, and
  the gathering's bin as the Removed view** — the surface for CK-66's
  backend (`decisions/2026-09-27-two-bins.md` §1–§4). `Remove from
  gathering` renders for the host on someone ELSE's `live`, `ready` row
  and on no other — a pending row keeps Decline (one act per state), the
  host's own rows keep CK-64's controls, a non-host is offered nothing —
  behind a deliberate second step: *"Nobody in this gathering will see
  this photo, {who} included. You can put it back for 30 days."*, Remove
  it / Leave it, posting CK-54's `remove` through `binMedia`; the step
  shares a row with no other step. The **Removed view** is a third view
  on the same list (`removed=true`, never both view flags, composing
  with `q`), rendered for the host only — the view switch itself now
  renders for the host always, and a non-host gets no switch at all —
  with its own empty state ("Nothing has been removed from this
  gathering.") and the host's bin line per row ("Removed from this
  gathering. Only you can see it, and you can put it back for N more
  days." — the personal line's clock, forms and ban). **Put back** is
  one click to `restore` — a host restore goes `live`, so the hint says
  everyone will see it again — and **Delete permanently** is CK-64's
  step with the same constant and sentence, to `destroy`, one photograph
  at a time; no bulk empty, no multi-select, no notice to the uploader
  (two-bins §8, open). The decline-on-a-live-row refusal now points at
  Remove from gathering instead of claiming the takedown "isn't possible
  here yet". Detail: `reference/frontend/components.md` 1.22.0, the
  Photographs convention's host-side bullets. *(Restated at CK-69: every
  part of this side renders for an ORGANISER — the host or a co-host, from
  the body's `caller_role` — except Delete permanently on someone else's
  row, which stays the host's; the Remove step's sentence follows the seat
  and the gate (`takedownSentence`), the bin line names its readers, a
  co-host's Put back hint says the host will look again where the
  gathering requires approval, and every row in the Removed view is read
  as a gathering-bin row — the CK-69 bullet below.)*
- Paid tiers, the backend (CK-73; migration 0029): **two photo plans on
  Stripe — 100,000 photographs at $4.99 a month, 300,000 at $9.99 — sold
  through Stripe-hosted Checkout and the Customer Portal, and the
  allowance read from a local row** (`decisions/2026-10-10-paid-tiers-on-stripe.md`;
  the frontend is CK-74). **Stripe owns the money; CoveyKeep owns the
  allowance**: `GET /billing`, `POST /billing/checkout` (409
  `already_subscribed` for an account already holding a paid plan),
  `POST /billing/portal` (404 with no customer) and the unauthenticated
  `POST /billing/stripe/webhook` — the signature verified over the raw
  body, the subscription FETCHED by id for the four handled types and
  upserted into `subscriptions` (the nudge, never the payload; out-of-order
  and duplicate delivery converge on Stripe's current state), one INFO
  line per handled event carrying the type and id and nothing else.
  **`keeping.account_quota` reads the row and never calls Stripe** —
  `active` and `past_due` are paid (a card Stripe is still retrying keeps
  the plan; revenue recovery cancels after the final retry, and `canceled`
  is the free tier with nothing frozen, hidden or deleted), every other
  status is the free tier; no allowance is stored anywhere;
  `_enforce_limits` has no diff; the quota path, the upload path and the
  worker never import `stripe`. **Deleting an account reaches Stripe
  first**: the customer deleted — which cancels its subscriptions at once
  — BEFORE the anonymization transaction, refused with a 503
  `billing_unavailable` and nothing anonymized if Stripe cannot be reached,
  the two billing rows deleted in the transaction. **Four new dashboard
  values on `covey-keep-api` ALONE, required with no default** —
  `STRIPE_API_KEY` (a RESTRICTED sandbox key, boot-checked to start
  `rk_test_`: a full-access `sk_` key and a live `rk_live_` key each refuse
  to boot naming the field), `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (`whsec_`),
  `STRIPE_PRICE_LOOKUP_100K`, `STRIPE_PRICE_LOOKUP_300K` — set in the
  dashboard BEFORE the push that declares them (the SESSION_SECRET / R2
  ordering: a push made first crash-loops the API naming the first missing
  `stripe_*` field, fixed in the dashboard and never by relaxing the check),
  never on the worker, which gains nothing. The webhook endpoint is
  registered at `https://covey-keep-api.onrender.com/billing/stripe/webhook`
  against the SDK's API version, `2026-09-30.endive` (`stripe==16.0.0`).
  **Test cards only, in the sandbox; live mode is its own gate** (record
  §13 — the boot check refuses a live key until it is discharged). A
  Checkout or portal URL is a bearer credential for a payment surface and
  is never logged; the `stripe` logger is held at WARNING. Not built: the
  frontend (Settings → Your plan, `/plan` — CK-74), the keeping flows that
  read `holds_paid_plan`, annual prices, a third plan, a trial, metering, a
  retention offer, notifications, live mode. Detail:
  `reference/backend/api-reference.md` (Billing router),
  `reference/backend/database-schema.md` migration 0029 and decision 45,
  `reference/operations/deploy.md` (Stripe env surface). *(Restated at CK-73.1: `GET /billing` carries `ends_at` — when the plan ends if it is ending, derived from `cancel_at` else the period end when the boolean is true — in place of `cancel_at_period_end`, because under `2026-09-30.endive` the portal cancels through `cancel_at` with the boolean left false (migration 0030 stores it beside the boolean, nothing backfilled); the webhook answers a subscription Stripe no longer holds with a 200 and one ERROR line, never the 503; and the API's own log lines reach the deploy for the first time — `app/logging_setup.py`, the Gotcha below.)* *(Restated at CK-73.2: a downgrade waiting for renewal is stored and shown — migration 0031's `scheduled_plan` and `scheduled_plan_at`, the plan of the subscription schedule's next phase and the instant it starts, written by the upsert from the one fetch's four-level expansion and never read by the allowance; `GET /billing` carries `scheduled_change` — the next plan, its allowance from the one plan table and the instant — null whenever `ends_at` is set; the webhook handles the six `subscription_schedule.*` events as the same nudge, because a schedule can change without any field on the subscription changing; the restricted key needed nothing.)* *(Restated at CK-74: the frontend half is BUILT — `/plan` and the Settings section, every number from `GET /billing`, which now carries `used` (the caller's own charged count) and `plans` (the three offers with the prices from `keeping.PLAN_MONTHLY_PRICE_CENTS`) and still makes no Stripe call; the keeper's own room refusal carries `keeper_space_full` and the upload form's Get more space link to `/plan` hangs on it; the return from Checkout is a wait on `/plan?checkout=complete`, never an error.)* *(Restated at CK-74.1: every Checkout Session carries `allowed_payment_method_types: ["card"]` — cards only, Stripe Link OFF by Steven's decision of 2026-10-21, in code so the sandbox and live cannot drift and nothing in either Dashboard needs setting; Apple Pay and Google Pay ride `card` and stay. And the plan screen says only what is true: no cancel line on an ending plan's Free card, Try again on a failed read, the waiting page with its heading and way back.)* *(Restated at CK-74.2: the filter alone did NOT turn Link off — check `(jh)`, 2026-10-22, found the pre-ticked Link box, its phone-number field and the Link wallet button still offered under it, with Cash App Pay, Klarna and Amazon Pay gone — so every Checkout Session now also carries `wallet_options: {"link": {"display": "never"}}`, the SDK's own setting for whether Checkout displays Link; the card-only filter stays as Steven's decision of 2026-10-22, standing on its own: cards with Apple Pay and Google Pay cover nearly everyone, the list is fixed in code so sandbox and live match, and bank payments and buy-now-pay-later add ways for a $4.99 monthly payment to fail without adding buyers. `(jk)` is the look at the sandbox that decides it; a parameter's documentation never did.)*
- Your groups (CK-78; frontend only): **Groups 3a — the list, a group's page,
  its members, and running it**, the screens for CK-45's and CK-75's backend
  (`decisions/2026-10-23-joining-a-group-and-co-admins.md` §5–§7). Three
  routes behind `RequireAuth`, linked from Home's *Your groups*: `/groups`
  (one request; name, count, the caller's role; a real empty state; Try
  again on a failed read), `/groups/new` (one field, **no type control**,
  `group_type` never sent) and `/groups/:id` (the member list with initials,
  renaming, co-admins, removal, handing over and leaving). **The role is
  the body's `caller_role` through `lib/groups.ts::groupRoleFlags`** —
  `isAdmin` for the reserved acts, `organises` for the delegable ones,
  anything unknown closed; no surface compares person ids. Every act but
  Make co-admin takes a second step inline on its row; a refusal lands on
  its row from its code; a 404 on either read is one not-found screen;
  nothing identifying is ever rendered — the membership id is the handle in
  calls alone. **`/groups/join` is a static row rendering the catch-all**,
  because `/groups/:id` would otherwise capture CK-75's join URL and, behind
  `RequireAuth`, drop its fragment on the way to `/` — Groups 3b replaces
  the row with the landing screen. Not built: joining — the link, emailed
  invitations, the request queue, the two landing screens (3b); a group's
  gatherings, the places list, the photos-only view (3c); notifications; the
  claim flow. Detail: `reference/frontend/components.md` 1.30.0, the Groups
  on the surface convention. *(Restated at CK-79: the static `/groups/join`
  row is replaced by the link's landing, and joining is built — the bullet
  below. Restated at CK-80: 3c is built too — a group's gatherings, the
  places, the photos-only view — the bullet after it.)*

## Deploys bullets restated at 1.91.0, as they stood in `CLAUDE.md` v1.90.0

The three Deploys bullets that carried no amendment mark, so the 1.89.0 rewrite did not reach them, verbatim and in their original order, as they stood before `CLAUDE.md` 1.91.0 (2026-10-29) rewrote each to say the current state; the other bullets stayed where they were.

- The publication gate (CK-41; migration 0019): **whether a photograph
  waits for the host is a default the product sets and the host owns,
  resolved through a ladder at publish time and never frozen at create**
  (`app/services/publication.py`; `decisions/2026-09-09-consent-gate-defaults.md`
  2.0.0 §1): the gathering's own setting (`gatherings.requires_approval` —
  the host's choice, written since CK-44 through `PATCH /gatherings/{id}`'s
  `requires_approval_override`), else the home group's default
  (empty today), else the type's template (`capability_profiles.requires_approval`,
  reachable only through a home group; empty today), else the join shape
  (privately joined does not wait; a link anyone can use does — the share
  link will be its producer), with an ORGANIZATION host gated as a
  backstop beneath the two rungs a person sets. **Every gathering today
  resolves open**, so the worker moves `pending → live` in the same
  guarded statement that moves `status` to `ready`; a gathering that
  resolves open is **unreviewed**, and no copy may call it safe, screened,
  reviewed or consent-verified. **`requires_approval` is nullable with no
  server default — NULL means inherit — and creation writes nothing to
  it** (the deliberate inversion of database-schema decision 22; do not
  "fix" the create path back). Every gathering body carries
  `requires_approval` as the EFFECTIVE value beside
  `requires_approval_override`, the host's own — **readable and not
  writable until CK-44, which ships after CK-43's review surface — built;
  writable since CK-44** (`true` / `false` / an explicit `null` = inherit;
  the effective value still refused — the switch bullet below). The
  resolver's `source` is not exposed — CK-44 did not need it (what the
  accuracy statement needs is the inherited answer, and today every
  gathering the switch can render on inherits open; the trigger is the
  share link or rung 2). `_visible_media` is untouched.
  No `groups` column, no belongs-to link, no share link, no profile rows.
- Groups (CK-45; migration 0021): **the first surface on a table that has
  existed since 0001 with no writer** — `app/api/groups.py`, the ninth
  router (`decisions/2026-09-14-groups-get-a-surface.md`). `POST /groups`
  creates a **HOUSEHOLD** group — and only that: `TEAM`, `CONGREGATION`
  and `CLUB` are schema-valid and endpoint-refused (a field-level 422
  naming rung 2 of the publication ladder — the CK-25 `SMS` shape, binding
  until the home group's default exists; `capability_profiles` keeps its
  one seeded row and the router seeds nothing). **The creator is admin
  and first member in one transaction** — a group never exists with zero
  members, even transiently (verifier-asserted on every deployed row) —
  with the membership's `household_id`, `sub_group_id` and `role_id` left
  NULL: no reader consumes them, and one of the seeded rungs is
  `observer`, the retired word. `GET /groups` (member-or-admin, one
  statement), `GET /groups/{id}`, `PATCH /groups/{id}` (rename, admin only;
  `name` never clearable; `updated_at` stamped by patches alone).
  **Both authorization checks live on the PERSON spine** —
  `groups.admin_person_id`, `memberships.person_id` — and the gatherings
  rule (an account fact plus a person fact) does not transfer; do not reach
  for `account_id` here. 404-not-403 byte-identical to a missing id;
  `member_count` computed at read time; **no body carries a roster**, an
  email, or a person id beyond the two admin columns; `name` is never
  logged. **No delete, no leave**: `gathering_invitations.group_id`
  carries no delete rule, so a group delete would 500 exactly as
  `delete_occurrence` did before 0015 — the rule is the delete phase's.
  **`POST /me/delete` relinquishes group admin** (both columns NULL
  wherever the person held them, the backup never promoted) **and keeps
  the membership** — `services/groups.py`, beside the kept-status lapse.
  Not built: membership of anyone else, invitations, sub-groups, the
  belongs-to link and the group default (the next phase), the thread.
- The sweep (CK-59; no migration): **Phase B — the bin's automatic half,
  shipped DARK.** `services/destruction.py::sweep_expired_bin` finds the
  `ready` + `removed` rows whose `removed_at` is at or past
  `now − REMOVED_BIN` — the same constant `_visible_media` reads, so the
  two clocks the bin record §6.1 names meet at one instant: nothing is
  swept while someone could still see it, and nothing sits
  invisible-and-still-charged past the window — and calls
  `mark_for_destruction` on each **row by row, never a bulk UPDATE**,
  committing per row: the statement's own `ready` guard is the
  concurrency safety (two workers cannot double-decrement) and it only
  works per row. **It marks; the CK-54 worker step destroys** — no
  second routine, no second decrement, no `delete_published_object` call
  here. **No memorial branch, no type branch, no account branch, and NO
  FIXTURE EXEMPTION** — no id allowlist, no date floor (bin record §7.4,
  §8; each decided). **The worker runs it only when `SWEEP_ENABLED` is
  on** — `WorkerSettings.sweep_enabled: bool = False`, the seventh
  worker variable, `"false"` in `render.yaml` until CK-73.2 and `"true"`
  since — ON on the deploy since `(gt)`, 2026-10-19 — turned on by hand in the
  worker's dashboard with no deploy — and only from an idle poll (the
  queue drains first, always), once per `SWEEP_INTERVAL` (15 min; the
  first pass at the first idle poll after boot), at most `SWEEP_BATCH`
  (20) rows a pass, because every mark is a destroy job claimed AHEAD of
  any fresh upload (`claim_next` orders by `created_at` across both
  ladders) and twenty bounds that at ≈11 s; a pass that marked anything
  is drained before the loop sleeps, so `--once` with the switch on is
  the operator's sweep from a shell. One INFO line per pass that marked
  anything — the count, nothing else; silence otherwise. **The module
  boundary is the caller set**: `destruction.py` holds what the API and
  the worker both call and never imports `ingest`, `processing`,
  `storage` or botocore (the Gotcha below); `handle_destroying` stays in
  `ingest.py`. **The two decline rows** (`ck43-eg-decline.png`,
  `ck44-em-decline.png`, `removed_at` 2026-09-14, the project's only
  decline evidence) are captured in CK-59's report as far as this
  machine could reach them; the psql row state is Steven's BEFORE the
  switch, and they go on the first pass at or after **2026-10-14** with
  it on. Not built: Phase C (the bin surface, empty-the-bin, every
  user-facing quota surface), restore-from-bin, a per-account bin
  ceiling; CK-57 (what a `destroying` row's `last_error` means) should
  follow, because the sweep makes a stuck destruction likely rather than
  theoretical once it is on.
